# Market research council, 2026-11-01: the full record

Status: complete (five passes). Requested by the owner on 2026-11-01; process in `team/direction-councils.md`
("Market research council"). Proposals go to the idea board (`team/idea-board.md`); nothing here is a decision.

**What this is.** A council of AI agents (one subagent per member, three competitive analysts, three fact-checkers) researched
the market for Stock Social in five passes: (1) independent desk research with sources, (2) cross-examination, each member
challenging two others, (3) a fact-check of the numbers and claims the conclusions rest on, (4) the chair's synthesis, (5) a
red-team pass on the synthesis and the chair's final revision. It is market research, not legal, tax or investment advice.

**Read first:** Part A, the chair's final synthesis (the ten findings are its §1; the proposals its §13, repeated at the end of
this file). Parts B to F are the record behind it, kept in full so every claim can be traced.

**Ground rules applied throughout.** Third parties are described generically (POLICY T1, T2, T4); sources are cited by
publisher type, date and, where the address carries no third-party brand, URL. Nothing invented is presented as data: every
number has an opened source, or is labelled *estimate* (arithmetic shown) or *assumption*. Persona walkthroughs are labelled
SIMULATION and are not research data. No interviews or surveys were run; DESIGN.md's mention of "interviews of ICP-S1" has no
record in the repository and is treated as unverified. Where a later pass corrects an earlier one, the later pass wins: the
fact-checks (Part D) override pass 1 and 2 claims, and Part A reflects them. Citation prefixes in Part A point to the member
files (e.g. [DA-…] data analyst, [RT-…] red team, [FC-M…], [FC-R…], [FC-L…], [FC-K…] the market, research, legal and code
fact-checks).

**Inputs.** The repository at commit dff076b (POLICY, DESIGN, COMPLIANCE, DECISIONS, FEATURES, F-1..F-6, IDEAS, the idea board),
and the owner's projection dashboard of 2026-10-25 (its 17 assumptions and benchmark list, reproduced generically in the data
analyst's file). The data analyst's model is in Appendix G and, with the dashboard's assumptions and the council brief, in `docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/` (run `node docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/model.mjs`). The dashboard's benchmark list is not committed because its source links carry third-party names; the data analyst's and fact-checkers' files describe each entry generically. Code claims were checked at commit 15ca6c3; nothing under `lib/`, `app/` or `db/` changed between it and dff076b.

## Contents
- Part A. Chair's final synthesis (pass 5)
- Part B. Pass 5: red-team review of the synthesis
- Part C. Pass 4: chair's first synthesis (superseded by Part A; kept for the record)
- Part D. Pass 3: fact-checks (research; market; legal and code)
- Part E. Pass 2: cross-examinations (11 members)
- Part F. Pass 1: desk research (11 members)
- Appendix G. The data analyst's model
- Proposals for the idea board

# Part A. Chair's final synthesis (pass 5)

## Market research council, pass 5: chair's final synthesis

**Member:** chair. **Pass:** 5 (final revision of p4-chair-synthesis.md after the red team's p5-red-team.md).
**Date:** 2026-11-01.
**Inputs:** docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/BRIEF.md; 11 pass-1 files; 11 pass-2 files; 3 pass-3 fact-checks, which override earlier claims they
correct; the red team's 26 required and 6 optional changes. Repo: DESIGN §1.2 and §3.5, F-3, POLICY, COMPLIANCE §1.
I re-ran `docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/model.mjs` and added the arithmetic shown. No new web sources were opened.

This is market research. It is not legal or investment advice, and it recommends no security or trade. DESIGN's
"interviews of ICP-S1" have no record and are treated as unverified. Every change from p4 is listed in §14.

**Labels.**
- **FACT**: a source opened by a member, re-verified in pass 3 where marked.
- **ESTIMATE**: arithmetic on facts plus stated assumptions.
- **OPINION/HYPOTHESIS**: judgment.
- Confidence: High / Medium / Low.

**Source ids.**
- `[XX-Cn]` is claim n in p1-XX; `[XX-Sn]` is a source in that file; `[XX-P2]` is that member's pass-2 file.
- `[FC-L#]`/`[FC-K#]` are legal and code fact-check rows; `[FC-M#]` market rows; `[FC-R#]` research rows.
- `[RT5-#]` is a red-team pass-5 item.

**Member keys.** DA data analyst, MS market sizing, PR pricing, UT traders researcher, UV viewers researcher,
DI distribution, RT red team, REG regulatory, BT broker-native/trackers/vendor, CCS copy and signals,
SF social and forums.

---

### 1. Executive summary: the ten findings

1. **Break-even depends on traders per viewer.** At scale, break-even conversion = (trader_share × $1 + $0.004) ÷ $8.80.
   - Dashboard ratio (10%): 1.18%.
   - Funnel ratio (1.5-4.5%): 0.2-0.6%.
   - Gate-implied ratio (0.23-0.69%): 0.07-0.12%.
   - Lower conversion and lower trader share partly offset each other. The owner's "1.7-2.8%" is only the 1,000-viewer
     case. [DA-C1, DA-P2, RT5-1] (High arithmetic; Low inputs)
2. **The two deciding inputs are unmeasured, and the dashboard's ranges are probably off in opposite directions.**
   - Conversion is probably lower than the 1.5% base. The council's prior is 0.1-0.5% as a stock, and that prior is
     weakly anchored.
   - Trader share is possibly far lower than 10%.
   - Each payer carries at most ~8.5 traders at 1.5% conversion, or 5-8 at the prior (fixed costs excluded).
   [PR-C3, DA-C5, DA-C9, RT5-2, RT5-5] (Medium)
3. **One week-8 gate is not instrumented as built, and two are proxies.** There is no referrer or view capture. D7
   covers signed-in viewers only. Share events have a dead column and an unrecorded button. A few engineering days fix
   this. [FC-K1..K6] (High)
4. **At n=50 the owner's 60% share gate is a coin flip.** A cohort that truly shares at 60% passes 56% of the time; at
   55% it passes 29%. The gates are correlated. Viewers per trader are tested only implicitly, and nothing tests
   payment intent. [RT-C9, RT5-6] (High)
5. **As designed, the hourly sync would exceed the vendor's polling guidance once the owner turns the cron on.**
   - Up to 24× on activities and 6× on positions.
   - Nothing calls it today.
   - Transactions gain nothing from hourly polling; for positions the gain is unclear.
   [FC-M18, FC-K8, RT5-11] (High)
6. **The vendor terms (2026-05-22) may cover parts of the design.**
   - Read literally, they may cover stored open-position percentages, public display and the paid tier.
   - The same literal reading may reach closed-trade returns too.
   - Only the vendor can say. Termination is at will, so a written answer lowers this risk but cannot remove it.
   [FC-M8..M13, RT5-12] (High on the text; Low on how it is read)
7. **"Verified" and "multi-broker" no longer set the product apart.** A commission-free app broker opened a free,
   identity-verified feed to all eligible US customers. Its posters choose which trades to post, and profiles can be
   shared off-platform.
   - Among the patterns the council found, no US-equity product combines no per-trade hiding, losses ranked like gains,
     intervals, lower-bound ranking, freeze on disconnect and percent-only display.
   [FC-M19..M21, BT-C8, RT5-14] (Medium-Low)
8. **Many records will probably look unflattering over long windows, but transfer of the evidence is weak.**
   - The research covers day traders and finfluencers' recommendations.
   - Hypothesis: the disposition effect makes closed-trade win rates look better than whole records. If so, open
     positions (TR-4) become the crux of trust.
   [FC-R19, FC-R21, RT5-15] (Low on transfer)
9. **The paying job is narrow.** The record is free, so "check before I pay" brings traffic, not revenue. Only repeat
   follower-choosers plausibly pay. The channel audience holds smaller portfolios. $9.99 looks like a ceiling (opinion;
   the comparable prices are unverified). [PR-C4, PR-P2, FC-R5, FC-M28] (Low-Medium)
10. **Distribution has to be founder-led and image-first.**
    - Link posts get 1.1% of feed views and are 4-7× less visible.
    - Paid ads lose money at plausible inputs ($65-119 of contribution against a stale $150 acquisition cost).
    - POLICY's ban on third-party trackers (P6) removes ad optimisation.
    - On assumed rates the views gate misses by about 1.5-50×, depending on cohort mix.
    [FC-R29, FC-R30, DA-C10, RT5-3, RT5-16, RT5-17] (Medium on direction; Low on size)

### 2. Segments and jobs to be done

#### 2.1 Traders who would publish

**FACT (High), narrow scope.**
- In one emerging market, 97% of persistent futures day traders lost money and 0.4% out-earned a bank teller [FC-R18].
- On one Asian exchange (1992-2006), fewer than 1% of day traders predictably earned positive returns net of fees
  [FC-R19].
- The most active of 66,465 US households (1991-96) earned 11.4% a year against the market's 17.9% [FC-R25].
- Of ~29,000 finfluencers, 28% were skilled, 16% unskilled and 56% antiskilled. The study scores their
  *recommendations*, not their trades. The antiskilled have more followers [FC-R21].
- Options: 21% of investors have traded them, and 43% of investors aged 18-34. The sync skips options
  [FC-R16, UT-P2].

**OPINION/HYPOTHESIS (Low on transfer) [UT-C1, RT5-15].**
- The evidence points toward many records reading "still early" or showing losses. None of it was measured on this
  product's users (US equities and ETFs).
- Counter-hypothesis (not yet sourced in this council): the disposition effect, realising gains sooner than losses,
  predicts that *closed*-trade win rates look better while losses sit in open positions. That would raise card share
  rates and make TR-4 the crux of trust.
- TR-1..TR-8 stop cherry-picking of trades, but not of traders: who connects, and when [RT-C2, UT-P2].

| Segment | Job | What serves it | What works against it |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 signals seller (DESIGN ICP-S1) | Prove I'm real so buyers pay | Verified label, full history, card | Record shows bad months; forward alerts barred (F3b); counsel topic open; other venues *pay* traders [CCS-C10, FC-M25] |
| S2 proud hobbyist (ICP-S2) | Status | Chart, card, feed reach | Must show the whole record (TR-1); options missing; ranking floor of 10 trades; an in-app broker feed lets customers choose which trades to post, at no cost [UT-C8, FC-M21] |
| S3 accountability/process trader (proposed) | A public record I can't edit | Freeze, no hiding, intervals | Small audiences, so few external views [UT-C8] |
| S4 long-history steady investor (proposed) | Show a calm, long record | "Long record" tier | Trades rarely; the feed favours big moves |

**The realistic first 50 (OPINION, Low-Medium) [UT-C10, DI-P2, SF-P2].** In order:
1. Traders who already publish full statements by hand.
2. Accountability traders.
3. Long-history investors.
4. Small transparency-led sellers, held until the legal committee rules on "paid signal sellers as users".
5. S2 traders with a good run, recruited one to one.

All must be adults with a supported broker.

#### 2.2 What makes a record worth sharing, and what happens to mediocre ones

**HYPOTHESIS (Low) [UT-C9, UT-P2].** Shares follow the chart's shape and sign, not the interval. Losing or flat records
get shared less often, in three ways:
- as proof of honesty ("I show my losses");
- as self-deprecating humour (entertainment as a motive: 59% of users vs 18% of non-users [FC-R6]);
- by accountability traders.

UT's 55% share-rate estimate used an unverifiable "15% edge" figure [FC-R20]. On a "40% green at first sync" assumption
the rate is 62%. Recruiting mix and market conditions decide the gate, not card design. Traders' fears (dollar amounts,
judgment on losses, method theft, link security) are hypothesis-grade [UT-C6]. A per-trade card ("one honest trade")
is still an F-4 open question.

#### 2.3 Viewers who would read, and who would pay

**FACT (High) [FC-R1..R7].**
- 29% of investors rely on social media or message boards for decisions; 60% of investors aged 18-34 do.
- 26% act on finfluencers' recommendations; 61% of investors aged 18-34 do.
- These users consult 7.6 sources vs 4.0 and check backgrounds more often (36% vs 14%). Yet 68-69% of those targeted
  by fraud lost money, against 26-29% of non-users.
- Reliance falls with portfolio size: 48% under $50K, 20% at $500K and above.
- A separate 2025 survey gives 24% of investors. Its age-band figure is not used here. Do not mix the two surveys.
- Short video is second-tier: 40% of followers use it, against 77% for long-form video [FC-R3, UV-P2].

**OPINION (Medium) [UV-C8, PR-P2].**
- The *casual scroller* is the largest group and will not pay.
- The *pre-purchase checker* gets the full answer free, and the person they want to check is usually absent. This
  group brings traffic, not revenue [UV-C9].
- The *follower-chooser* is the only plausible buyer, likely on an annual plan. Its size is unmeasured.

Compare has to work on a phone: 24% of adults 18-29 are smartphone-dependent [FC-R32].

**What the business depends on (High) [MS-C15, MS-P2, DA-P2].** The ceiling is 3-4 orders of magnitude above any
plausible obtainable share, so it does not bind. The funnel does:
1. Viewers per connected trader. trader_share runs 0.2-15% (~70×), which moves break-even from 0.07% to 1.75%.
2. Conversion, as a stock.
3. Willingness to publish a record that shows losses.
4. External views per trader, which follow a heavy tail [DI-C3].
5. Vendor coverage of creators' brokers, and exclusion of simulated accounts [FC-M16].

MS's table of obtainable share swings more than 10× on viewers per trader, and it repeated the stock/flow error. Treat
it as indicative only.

### 4. Competitive pattern map (generic descriptions only)

| Pattern | Verified how | Who pays whom | Key facts | Threat to us |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Large social copy-trading broker | Its own ledger only | Platform pays traders up to 1.5%/yr of copied assets | ~3,300 program **members** ≈ 0.1% of funded accounts (end 2024); number paid undisclosed; 4,750 members at end 2025; first paid tier needs $400K copied ≈ $6K/yr; outcome-screened, exclusive [FC-M22..M25] | Low in the US; whether US residents can be paid is not stated [FC-M26, REG-P2] |
| Broker-native verified feed (commission-free app broker) | Own ledger plus identity check | Free | Open to all eligible US customers; customers pick which eligible trades to post; licensed professionals excluded; one tap starts a trade from a post; profiles "can be shared off-platform"; whether signed-out web visitors can view them is not stated [FC-M19..M21] | High to the *verified label*; Medium-Low to the trust package. "Fast to build, slow to clear compliance" [REG-P2] |
| Broker-native verified share links and forum layers (outside the US) | Own backend; white-label holdings layer | Free | Exist; scale unknown [BT-C3] | Medium; the white-label layer could spread across brokers |
| Linked-broker social investing apps | Broker linking; "verified" claimed | Viewer-paid, reportedly $8-$20/month | Price set **unverifiable** [FC-M28]; several allow selective hiding [BT-C5] | Medium: data pipes, not trust rules |
| Trade journals | Broker sync (counts may include file import) | Trader pays ~$20-$80/month | Range plausible, exact set unverifiable [FC-M29] | Fastest *capable* entrant [BT-C6, CCS-P2] |
| Portfolio trackers; disclosure-mirroring apps | Broker sync; lagged public filings | ~$7.50-$16/month; ~$10/month ($89.99/yr unsourced) | Holdings or filings focus [BT-C7, FC-M27] | Low (different jobs) |
| Signal and strategy marketplaces; crypto copy | Read-only investor password (forex); hypothetical history (strategy) | Subscribers pay sellers; marketplace keeps ~20% or 13-30%; crypto profit share 10-50% | [CCS-C5, C7, C8] | Most *motive* to add "linked account" badges [CCS-C13] |
| Paid author marketplaces | None | Readers pay authors | 150 authors, 11,000 subscribers, $5.2M/yr (**2017**); premium $299/yr [FC-M30, M31] | Same "pay for judgment" wallet |
| Microblogs, boards, creators | Screenshots | Ads, creator funds | Link posts 1.1% of feed views [FC-R29] | Complement: bio link, reply, thread |
| Forex verified-record services | Read-only terminal password, since 2009; whole-account histories not editable trade by trade | Unknown | 2010 source [BT-C8] | The whole-account pattern exists, but in another asset class; built to sell copying |

**What may be defensible (OPINION, Medium-Low) [BT synthesis, CCS-P2, RT5-14].** Among the patterns the members found,
no US-equity product combines the full trust package:
- no per-trade hiding;
- losses ranked like gains;
- intervals and lower-bound ranking;
- freeze on disconnect;
- percent-only display;
- unpaid traders, adults only, and a signed-out open-web link.

This is an absence claim built from a sample, not a survey of the market. Forex verifiers already show whole-account
histories, so the pattern exists but has not spread to equities. Policy choices are cheap to copy for any firm without
a conflicting revenue model. What would make them durable is accumulated history: records frozen from early on, and
rankings that need long records. Firms with a conflicting model (selective posting, copy fees) have reasons not to copy
[CCS-C13].

**Not defensible:** "verified" (free at scale), "multi-broker" (table stakes), the share card.

---

### 5. Willingness to pay for Record tools ($9.99 / $99.99)

**FACT.**
- North America app medians are $9.99 monthly and $39.99 yearly, across all categories [FC-M34].
- After 12 months, 44.1% of yearly and 17.0% of monthly plans are still paying; lower-priced plans keep more
  [FC-M35, M36].
- Day-35 download-to-paid is 2.1% for freemium and 10.7% for hard paywalls [FC-M32].
- 40% of North American plans sold are yearly [FC-M33].
- Card fees come to 7.1% on $9.99 [FC-M39].
- 70% of social-media-using investors *rely on* paid newsletters or online resources (40% of all investors). That
  measures reliance, not payment [FC-R4].

**ESTIMATE: break-even at scale as a function of trader share [DA-C1, DA-P2, RT5-1].**
BE conversion = (trader_share × $1 + $0.004 hosting) ÷ $8.80 net per payer. Fixed costs are excluded.

| Traders per monthly viewer (source) | Viewers per trader | BE conversion at scale |
|---|---|---|
| 10% (dashboard base) | 10 | 1.18% |
| 5% / 15% (dashboard strong / conservative inputs) | 20 / 7 | 0.61% / 1.75% |
| 1.5-4.5% (DI funnel, MS-P2) | 22-67 | 0.22-0.56% |
| 0.23-0.69% (implied by the views gate) | 144-433 | 0.07-0.12% |

**The offset.** The council's lower conversion prior (0.1-0.5%) and the lower gate-implied trader share pull in
opposite directions. The pessimistic conversion only sinks the business if trader share stays near the dashboard's
5-15%. The same rule, read the other way, gives the minimum viewers per trader for a given conversion:

| Conversion (stock) | Max trader_share | Min viewers per trader | Max traders per payer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1% | 0.48% | 208 | 4.8 |
| 0.25% | 1.80% | 56 | 7.2 |
| 0.5% | 4.00% | 25 | 8.0 |
| 1.5% | 12.8% | 8 | 8.5 |

Break-even with fixed costs, base case: 2.89% at 1k viewers, 1.35% at 10k, 1.20% at 100k. The conservative case is
5.58% at 1k. At the gate-mid ratio, $9.99 breaks even at 0.25% from ~11.2k monthly uniques, with profit at gate
traffic of −$148/−$4/+$234 at 0.1/0.25/0.5%. $4.99/$39.99 nets $4.03 and needs ~2.2× the conversion [DA-P2].

**Stock vs flow (ESTIMATE, Low) [DA-C5, RT5-4, RT5-5].** stock = monthly flow × (new ÷ monthly uniques) × paying life.
Paying life is 12.2-16.8 months, blended across plans, between monthly-plan churn of 13.7% (implied by 17% one-year
retention of *monthly* plans) and the dashboard's 7%. With 60% new viewers (assumption), the council's 0.1-0.5% stock
implies a monthly flow of only **≈0.01-0.07% of new viewers paying**, far below every published benchmark. The prior is
least anchored here. Either share-link viewers convert far worse than app installs, or the stock is too low. Only
measurement can tell.

**OPINION (Medium) [PR-C4, PR-C9, UV-P2].** Record tools is convenience over free data. Willingness to pay is probably
bimodal: near zero for most viewers, high for the few who already pay for judgment. The $8.99-$19.99 viewer prices of
linked apps are unverified [FC-M28], so "$9.99 is a ceiling" is OPINION, Low.

**Packaging options within POLICY.** None gates the record, charges traders, sells visibility or pushes anything.
1. Keep $9.99/$99.99 as the ceiling and test one lower arm. The lower arm wins only if conversion rises more than 2.2×.
   Showing any price other than the owner's needs the owner (§13 #6).
2. Annual-first presentation. $99.99 is 16.6% off twelve monthly payments [PR-C2].
3. A free limited compare (two traders, one saved view). Needs the legal committee [PR-C5].
4. A card-up-front 7-day trial, or a 14-day money-back arm. Evidence that a 7-day trial is best: **abstract only,
   unverified** [UV-P2, RT5-25].
5. A "supporter" plan with no visible badge, or a club plan (legal review).

Excluded by rule: alerts (F3b), paid follow or watchlist limits, boosted profiles (F5), paid open positions or theses,
and anything charged to traders.

Auto-renewal: the vacated click-to-cancel rule is back in rulemaking (ANPRM, 13 Mar 2026). ROSCA and state laws still
apply, so the annual plan needs a pre-renewal notice with no record field [FC-L23, PR-C7].

---

### 6. Distribution channels within POLICY

**FACT.**
- Link posts made up 1.1% of US feed views on a large general network in Q2 2026 [FC-R29].
- On a microblog, links to non-news external sites cut median visibility by ~4.0-6.8× (observational) [FC-R30].
- "59% never clicked" is directional only [FC-R28].
- The +246% broadcast effect ran inside the host network [SF-P2].
- The card image already carries the F3 disclaimer (`app/u/[handle]/record-card-image.tsx` renders `disclaimerLines`),
  so image-first posting is compatible with F3.

**ESTIMATE (Low): funnel arithmetic, all rates assumed [DI-C4, DI-C5, SF-P2, RT5-17].**
- Views per post = audience × share who see it × click-through.
  - Hobbyist with 1,000 followers: 2.
  - Seller with 10,000 followers: 20 at a 10% seen-rate. With the corrected non-news link penalty (seen 1.5-2.5%):
    3-5.
  - A 500-member chat group at 30%: 150.
- Low end: 30 sharers posting once a week at ~25 views gives 750 views, 15% of the gate. On penalised feed posts alone
  the gap reaches ~50×.
- High end: 34 chat or forum posts a week at 150 views would meet the gate. Bio links and the founder's own earned
  media add more.
- Net: **a miss of ~1.5-50× depending on mix.** The median sharer brings far fewer than the 100-a-week average.
- Founder cost: 600-1,300 contacts for 50 connects (1,000 × 20% × 40% × 50% = 40). Over 8 weeks that is
  **75-160 contacts a week**, essentially a full-time job for one founder.

**Measurement trap [DI-P2].** Apps often drop the link from an image share, so those visits arrive as "direct". A
first-party `?via=` parameter avoids depending on Referer.

| Best (in POLICY) | Why |
|---|---|
| Founder-led one-to-one recruitment of transparency-led traders (bio link, pinned post, their community) | Persistent; highest views per share [DI-C4, DI-P2] |
| Native card image, with the link in a reply, the bio, a chat or a forum thread | Images travel, links don't. Discussion boards reach 40% of adults 18-29 [SF-C11, FC-R31] |
| Earned media and education on reading records ("what a range means", "why losses show") | Screenshot problem documented by regulators; no completeness or fraud-detection claims (F3a) [DI-C7] |

| Worst | Why |
|---|---|
| Paid performance ads | Allowed by POLICY if ad copy meets F2/F3 and targets adults only (P4). But no third-party trackers or pixels (P6) means no optimisation loop. Contribution of $65-119 per payer at the gate-mid ratio is below a $150 acquisition cost built on stale 2021-22 per-registration costs (ESTIMATE, Low) [FC-M40, RT5-3, RT5-16] |
| Paid non-trader creators | Allowed with FTC disclosure and monitoring; FINRA entanglement if representatives are involved. Paying *traders* is barred (F5) [DI-C8, FC-L24] |
| Rewarded referrals | Barred for traders (F5/F6). A viewer reward is a material connection that must be disclosed [DI-C10] |

SEO is slow for week 8, and the sitemap lists no profiles [FC-K11].

---

### 7. Regulatory forces shaping the market (findings, not legal advice)

1. **The publisher's exclusion (472 U.S. 181)** protects impersonal, disinterested, regular publication and "factual
   information about past transactions" (pp. 206-210) [FC-L1..L5]. Quote the regularity passage in full [FC-L2].
   - OPINION (Medium/Low): it plausibly covers finished records.
   - It is weaker for live open positions, per-viewer ordering (F1a) and paid watch-style views, and it does not
     protect traders [RT-P2]. REG reads the fit more favourably [REG-C1].
2. **Compensation** means "any economic benefit" from anyone (IAC draft; IA-1092 unverifiable) [FC-L7]. F5 adds no
   compensation, but it removes none that traders already earn elsewhere [RT-P2].
3. **Enforcement targets the "successful trader" persona** [FC-L9..L11, L16, L17, L19..L21]:
   - eight charged, seven of them posing as successful traders, ~$100M;
   - more than 30 microcaps in a jury verdict;
   - false claims of more than $1B in assets under management;
   - a pool that promised to "post all trades" and took at least $10.1M.

   Real records rule out invented performance, not scalping. This shows fraud on the supply side, not willingness to
   pay [RT-P2].
4. **Finfluencer rules are open, not law.**
   - IAC draft recommendations [FC-L8].
   - FINRA RN 26-14 adds a supervision risk factor [FC-L27].
   - The sweep produced 5 actions, plus 3 related [FC-L25].
   - ESMA's "transparency of previous recommendations" line is not an influencer proposal [FC-L35].
5. **Firms are "entangled" with posts they arrange** [FC-L24]. Public broker feeds are costly to supervise, which
   favours an independent publisher [REG-P2]. An adviser posting a card likely needs total-portfolio context
   [FC-L15].
6. **Data access is vendor-mediated, by contract.**
   - The CFPB 1033 rule excludes investment accounts and is stayed, so there is no federal right to brokerage data and
     the price is set by contract [FC-L31].
   - The amended Reg S-P (incident response, 30-day customer notice, phased compliance) gives brokers a reason to
     restrict screen-scraping and favour vetted API partners [REG-C8, FC-L32].
   - Market structure: entry runs through one or two aggregators, and access can be priced or withdrawn.
7. **Privacy conditions sit on launch, not just on cost [COMPLIANCE §1, RT5-24].**
   - GLBA and the FTC Safeguards Rule treat brokerage data as financial data and require a written security program.
   - Several state privacy laws treat financial data as *sensitive*: consent at connect, plus access, deletion and
     correction rights.
   - POLICY P6 already states that "a record freeze never blocks deletion".
   - These conditions make deletion, export and documented consent part of the product, and they apply to any
     waitlist or analytics data too.
8. **Age assurance.** The holding in No. 23-1122 is limited to content obscene to minors. In No. 25A97 a concurrence
   notes seven district courts enjoining similar laws, so "easier to sustain" is unsupported [FC-L33, L34]. Treat it
   as a watch item.
9. **UK and EU.** FSMA s21 is criminal and reaches across borders [FC-L38]. A disclaimer "will not protect you"
   [FC-L36].
10. **FTC endorsement and review rules** make paid promotion costly. Unrewarded trader cards need no disclosure
    [REG-C11, FC-L22].

---

### 8. Platform risk (SnapTrade)

**FACT (High) on the text. Developer Terms, "Last Updated: May 22, 2026"** [FC-M7].

| Clause | Text | What it may reach (reading at FC confidence) |
|---|---|---|
| §2(b) | No "sell, license, or purchase any data obtained from SnapTrade" [FC-M8] | Possibly the paid tier, if tools over records count as selling data [BT-P2] |
| §2(d) | No re-packaging "to third parties" without written consent of **both** the End User and SnapTrade [FC-M9] | Public profiles, *if* public display counts as re-packaging to third parties |
| §2(f) | "will not **store**, resell, or redistribute Market Data"; the definition covers data "derived from data provided by any third party", prices included [FC-M10] | Stored `unrealizedPct`, computed from "Last known market price" [FC-K9]. FC-M10 leaves open whether a derived percentage is Market Data. Read literally, the definition may also reach closed-trade returns |
| §7(a) | Market data only "to serve the End User" [FC-M11] | Public open-position values; possibly public returns |
| §7(g) | Delete all End User data on request [FC-M12] | Consistent with POLICY P6. The remaining point is the survivorship skew if traders delete red records [REG-P2, RT5-13] |
| §4, §12 | Termination "at any time, for any reason"; 30 days' notice of price changes; the broker list can change without notice [FC-M13] | Dependence on one vendor that no written answer removes |

**Pricing (FACT).**
- $1 per connected user a month on the daily plan, $2 on real-time, plus $100 a month. Elsewhere the terms say "no
  monthly minimum"; ask [FC-M1, M2].
- Billing runs until **every** connection is deleted [FC-M4].
- The $0 OAuth offer probably does not apply to this product [FC-M5].
- A processing fee of up to 3% may be added [BT-P2].
- The $0.50 rate is unsupported [FC-M6].
- Manual sync costs $0.05, but the code never calls it [FC-M3].

**Polling (FACT, High; latent).**
- Guidance: activities at most once per account per 24h; positions at most 4 times a day. Transactions refresh daily,
  delayed a day, on both plans [FC-M18].
- As designed, the sync can claim each trader every hourly round and call both endpoints. Once the owner switches the
  cron on, that **would** run up to 24× and 6× over guidance.
- Nothing calls `/api/sync` today [FC-K8].
- Transactions gain nothing from hourly polling; for positions the gain is unclear [RT5-11].

**Coverage.** Copy should say "38 listed" (F3a) [FC-M14, M17]. Three simulated brokerages exist. The code drops
`is_paper` accounts, but whether the vendor sets that flag is unverified [FC-M16, FC-K7]. Crypto and forex are barely
covered [BT-P2].

**OPINION (chair).** The text *may* cover open-position storage, public display and the paid tier, and read literally,
closed returns too. Only the vendor can say. A written answer lowers the risk but does not remove it, because
termination is at will.

---

### 9. Week-8 kill gates: are they set right?

**Measurability in the code as built (FACT, High).**

| Owner's gate | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| ≥50 connected traders | Measurable | Non-demo users with a live connection; paper accounts excluded [FC-K7] |
| ≥60% share at least once | Proxy only | Three share columns are live; **`badge_copied_at` is dead**; the month-recap share is not recorded; failed shares count as "copied"; a copy is not a post [FC-K4..K6] |
| ≥5,000 external-referrer views a week | Not instrumented as built (days of work, T3) | No Referer handling; no view table; `landed` fires only when signup starts [FC-K1, K2] |
| Viewer D7 ≥15% | Rough, signed-in only | `last_seen_at` overwritten; signed-out viewers leave no trace [FC-K3] |

**Statistical power at the owner's thresholds (ESTIMATE, High).**

| True share rate | 50% | 55% | 60% | 65% | 70% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P(≥30/50) | 10% | 29% | 56% | 81% | 95% |

D7 at 15%: ±7.0 points at n=100 and ±4.9 at n=200. The owner's gates set no minimum cohort.

**Weaknesses.**
- **The gates are correlated.** Views and D7 both ride on share links, since F3b removes push. Sharing and views both
  depend on cohort mix [UV-P2, DI-P2].
- **Crawlers inflate views** [RT-C9].
- **What the gates do and don't test.** Viewers per trader are tested only implicitly: the views gate implies ~100
  views per trader a week. Nothing tests payment intent.
- **The stop action has no baseline.** "Re-interview ICP-S1" needs something to compare against.

**Proposed revisions (PROPOSALS; flag for owner).** These keep the owner's targets as the continue lines. Every change
in a target is stated, and definitions should be pre-registered before week 1.

| # | Owner's target | Proposal | Net change vs owner | P(continue) at the owner's target rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | ≥50 connected traders | Same, defined as adult, non-demo, non-paper, with ≥1 imported closed trade. Inconclusive at 35-49 | Stricter definition; target unchanged | n/a (a count) |
| G2 | ≥60% share at least once | **Keep ≥30/50 to continue**. Add an inconclusive band of 26-29 (extend 4 weeks to n≈100) and stop at ≤25. Fix the instrument; report copy and share separately, with a CI | Target unchanged; adds an extend zone | At a true 60%: continue 56%, inconclusive 34%, stop 10%. At 55%: 29% / 43% / 28% |
| G3 | ≥5,000 external-referrer views a week | **Keep 5,000 views a week**. Also report monthly unique external viewers (first-party, bot-filtered) and monthly uniques per connected trader. Owner's target implies 144-433 per trader; diagnostic economics floor ≥56 (break-even at 0.25% conversion) | Target unchanged. The per-trader floor of 56 would be 3-8× **looser** than the target's implied ratio, so it is shown only as a diagnostic unless the owner adopts it | Not a binomial; depends on the heavy tail |
| G4 | ≥15% D7 | Same threshold, on viewers who signed in or followed, cohort n≥200 (±4.9 points). Report all-visitor return when measurable | Target unchanged; cohort defined | At a true 15%, n=200: ~50% pass, ~2% fall below 10% |
| G5 | none | **New.** Payment intent: painted-door waitlist joins as a share of signed-in returning viewers (directional) | Added gate | n/a |
| G0 | none | **New.** A written vendor answer on §2(b), §2(d), §2(f), §7(a), §7(g) before real users | Added hard gate | n/a |

**G5 is directional only [RT5-9].** Converting it to the §5 stock needs:
implied stock ≈ G5 × (signed-in returning ÷ monthly uniques) × intent-to-pay discount.
The share of signed-in returners and the 10-50% discount are *assumptions*. Example: 3% × 20% × 25% ≈ 0.15%. Compare
the result with the §5 table only after real-money data replaces the discount.

**Proposed decision rule (flag for owner).** Keep the owner's "miss two or more" rule, and add three things:
- G0 and G1 as hard gates;
- an inconclusive result is not counted as a miss;
- the G3 per-trader number and G5 are reported alongside, as economics diagnostics.

### 10. The projection dashboard: verdict and corrections

**"Break-even at roughly 1.7-2.8% of monthly viewers paying": partly right, misleading as a headline (High).**
- It covers the strong and base cases at 1,000 viewers (1.78%, 2.89%) and omits the conservative case (5.58%).
- At scale, break-even is a function of trader share: 1.18% at the dashboard's 10%, 0.2-0.6% on the funnel ratio, and
  0.07-0.12% at the gate-implied ratio (§5).
- F-3's 2026-10-25 rationale quotes the 1k case.
- Better headline: "**break-even conversion ≈ (traders per viewer × broker fee + hosting) ÷ net revenue per payer;
  each payer carries 5-8.5 traders, scale case, fixed costs excluded.**"

**"Broker data about $1 per connected trader a month": the price is verified, the cost basis incomplete (High)**
[FC-M1..M6].
- Add billing until the last connection is deleted, and a possible 3% fee.
- Use $2 as the stress case.
- Drop $0.50. Never use $0 OAuth.

| Input | Problem | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| `conv` | Benchmarks are flows with committed denominators; "1-3% of free readers" unsourced [DA-C5, FC-M32, M41] | Define conv as a stock = flow × new share × paying life; add conv-by-price; flag the implied 0.01-0.07% flow |
| `price` | No scenario variation, so the tornado shows zero [PR-P2] | Add a price input |
| `trader_share` | 5-15% is assumed [DA-C9] | Add the gate (0.23-0.69%) and funnel (1.5-4.5%) scenarios; plot results along this axis |
| `churn_monthly` | 3.6% is **annual** in its source [FC-M38] | Drop it. 17% one-year retention of *monthly* plans implies ~13.7% a month blended (front-loaded); 7% is optimistic; PR-P2 suggests 10%. Paying life 12.2-16.8 months |
| `annual_share` | Global 34% | North America 40% [FC-M33] |
| `cac_paying` | Cost per registration from 2021-22 data, not installs [FC-M40] | Relabel; $150 is a stale estimate. Contribution per payer at the gate-mid ratio is $65-119 |
| b03, b06, b10, b20, b27 | Program members, not paid traders; $89.99 unsourced; 2017; annual; registration | Fix the text [FC-M note 5] |

**Measure first:** monthly unique external viewers per connected trader. Once billing runs, measure payers per trader.

### 11. Cheapest tests first

Ordered by cost. All thresholds are proposals.

| # | Test | Cost | Unlocks | Pass / fail, sample size |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Written questions to SnapTrade (§13 #1) | ~$0 | Launching as designed | Pass: written permission for public closed %, stored and public open %, and the paid tier. A refusal on any of them means redesign (TR-4/F-6, flag for owner). Termination at will remains |
| T2 | Sync cadence fix, before the cron is switched on | Hours | Vendor compliance | Logs show ≤1 activities call and ≤4 positions calls per account-day |
| T3 | Privacy-safe measurement build (§13 #3) | Days | Reading the gates | Definitions committed before week 1; synthetic visits counted; no IP, user agent or user id stored with views |
| T4 | Whole-record preview as the P6 disclosure (§13 #11) | Low | Whether honest supply is viable | **Qualitative** at ~30 traders (a 15/15 split gives a CI of ±36 points). A quantitative read needs ≥100 per arm. Publish the aggregate "previewed, didn't publish" count |
| T5 | Outreach to 100 transparency-led traders | 75-160 contacts a week of founder time | First-50 mix | ≥20% connect [RT-C3 bar]; the seller arm waits for the legal committee |
| T6 | 10-15 interviews per segment, themes only | Low | Re-interview baseline | Qualitative |
| T7 | Monthly uniques per connected trader, weekly | $0 after T3 | Unit economics | Diagnostic: ≥56 is consistent with 0.25% break-even; <25 fails even at 0.5% |
| T8 | Painted door (§13 #6 conditions) | Low | Willingness to pay; price | ≥1,000 signed-in returning viewers exposed per arm (±1.1 points at 3%). Pass at ≥3% joining, CI lower bound >1.5% |
| T9 | Price survey after a painted-door click | Low | Acceptable range | ≥200 per segment; stated values are upper bounds |
| T10 | Adult comprehension of the interval line | Low | F-4 copy | n≥100 (±7.8 points at 80%). Pass if the CI lower bound is ≥70% reading it as past results |
| T11 | After P6 and T1: real-money launch, no trial vs a trial or money-back arm | Medium | Real stock conversion | At or above the §5 threshold for the measured viewers-per-trader ratio |

### 12. Dissent and unresolved disagreements

- **Traders per viewer.** Owner: 5-15%. DA: 0.23-0.69% from the gates. MS-P2: 1.5-4.5%, and calls DA's inference
  circular. Measure it (T7).
- **Conversion.** Dashboard: 0.5/1.5/3%. PR: 0.1-0.5% as a stock. UV-P2: 0.25-0.5%. RT5-5: the implied flow is far
  below benchmarks. Unresolved.
- **ICP-S1.** DI-C4: recruit large sellers (DI later revised this). SF-P2, UV-C9, RT-C3: large sellers have the least
  incentive and the most exposure. UT-P2: small transparency sellers are viable.
- **The views gate.** DI: a miss of 3-10×. SF-P2: 20-50×. RT5-17: ~1.5-50× by mix. RT would not loosen gates; the
  chair keeps the owner's targets.
- **Fast follower.** BT: journals and linked apps (capability). CCS: marketplaces (motive). REG-P2: broker feeds (fast
  to build, slow to clear compliance).
- **The publisher's exclusion.** REG versus RT-P2. Open until the legal committee rules.
- **Freeze vs deletion.** P6 settles that deletion wins. Still open: how much survivorship skew deletions create.
- **Willingness to pay.** PR: $3-6. UV-P2: bimodal. BT-P2: viewer-paid apps exist, prices unverified.
- **Churn.** DA: 13.7% on monthly plans. MS-P2 and PR-P2: front-loaded, so 10% as base. Affects lifetime value only.
- **Demand from enforcement.** REG-C3: demand exists. RT-P2: the cases show supply-side fraud only.
- **Unflattering records.** UT and RT: the evidence points to losses. RT5-15: the disposition effect may make closed
  trades look flattering. Not yet sourced.
- **Market timing.** RT-C10: the cycle is fading. New investors fell from 21% to 8%. Non-retirement investing among
  adults 18-34 fell from 32% to 26% (corrected [FC-R9, R10]).
- **Settled during the passes.** F5 compensation (RT-P2 over REG-C2); the age-assurance trend (FC-L34); the $4.99 arm
  (PR accepted DA-P2).

# Part B. Pass 5: red-team review of the synthesis

### Market research council, pass 5: red team on the chair's synthesis

**Member:** red team. **Pass:** 5. **Date:** 2026-11-01. **Target:** p4-chair-synthesis.md.
**Method:** re-ran the model; recomputed binomials, CIs and trader-share tables; traced numbers to p1-p3; read POLICY,
F-3 and COMPLIANCE §1. No new web sources. Not legal or investment advice.

**Arithmetic that checks out:**
- $8.80 net ARPU and the 1.18% limit (0.104 ÷ 8.80).
- The 208/56/25/8 minimum-viewers table.
- 21,667 views a month, and 144-433 viewers per trader.
- Gate-mid results: about 11.2k uniques to break even; −$148/−$4/+$234.
- P(≥30/50) = 10/29/56/81/95%.
- D7 half-widths of ±7.0 and ±4.9.
- 24× and 6× polling.
- $31.30 on $150 (ratio 0.21).
- $4.03 net and ×2.18 for the $4.99 arm.
- The census rows.

**Brand scan:** no third-party names or brand URLs, except the borderline organisation name in item 26.

#### Required changes

**1. Exec #1, §10: the 1.18% floor uses the very input exec #2 rejects.**
Problem: 1.18% holds only at trader_share 10%. At the gate ratio the synthesis prefers, the floor is (0.0023 to 0.0069
+ 0.004) ÷ 8.80 = **0.07-0.12%**. On the DI funnel (1.5-4.5%) it is 0.22-0.56%. The headline overstates the hurdle by
up to ~10×, and the synthesis never says that lower conversion and lower trader share partly offset each other.
Fix: state the floor as a function of trader_share (0.07-0.12% gate, 0.2-0.6% DI, 1.18% dashboard), and note the
offset.

**2. Exec #1: "≈8.5 traders per payer" holds only at 1.5% conversion.**
Problem: hosting per payer is $0.004 ÷ conv. At the council's 0.1/0.25/0.5% prior, the cap is 4.8/7.2/8.0 traders,
before fixed costs.
Fix: "≈8.5 at 1.5%; 5-8 at the council's prior; scale case, fixed costs excluded."

**3. Exec #10: the "$31 per $150" rests on three rejected inputs.**
Problem: it uses trader_share 10%, 7% churn (called optimistic in §10) and a stale $150 CAC (FC-M40). At the gate-mid
ratio and 0.25-0.5% conversion, contribution is $5.36-7.08 a month, or **$65-119** over a 12.2-16.8-month life. The
conclusion survives (still below $150); the figure does not.
Fix: give the range, its basis, and an ESTIMATE, Low label.

**4. §10 churn: "13.7%/month blended" is wrong.**
Problem: 13.7% comes from 17% one-year retention of *monthly* plans (FC-M35). §5's 16.8-month life rests on 7% churn.
Fix: say "monthly plans". Show life as 12.2-16.8 months (13.7% to 7% churn).

**5. §5 stock vs flow: an implication is missing.**
Problem: a 0.1-0.5% stock with 60% new viewers and a 12-17-month life implies a monthly flow of only ≈0.01-0.07% of
new viewers. That is far below every benchmark.
Fix: state it, and flag this as where the prior is least anchored.

**6. Exec #4 vs §5: contradiction.**
Problem: exec #4 says no gate tests viewers per trader. §5 derives a "gate-implied ratio" from the views gate, which
implies ≈100 views per trader per week.
Fix: "tested only implicitly; nothing tests payment intent."

**7. §9 G2 silently raises the owner's 60% to 66%.**
Problem (recomputed): with continue at ≥33/50, a true 60% cohort continues only **24%** of the time. A true 55% cohort
stops 28% of the time. The synthesis calls 60% a coin flip, then makes the owner's own target fail three times in four.
Fix: keep ≥30 as continue and 26-29 as inconclusive, or show the owner these probabilities and flag the raise.

**8. §9 G3/G4: a large loosening on views, in mixed units.**
Problem: the original gate implies 144-433 monthly viewers per trader. G3's ≥50 is 3-9× looser. G4 "halves" the gate
but switches from views to weekly uniques. 2,500 uniques is 2,500-7,500 views, so it may be *stricter*. Weekly uniques
also don't add up to monthly uniques.
Fix: use a single unit (monthly uniques), state the net change against the original, and flag it for the owner.

**9. §9: "G3 × G6 is the economic test" is undefined.**
Problem: G6's denominator is signed-in returning viewers. §5's thresholds use monthly uniques. Waitlist intent
overstates actual payment.
Fix: implied stock = G6 × (signed-in returning ÷ uniques) × intent discount, where the discount is an assumption given
as a range. Otherwise call G6 directional.

**10. §11 T4/T8/T10: samples too small to read the thresholds.**
Problem: at ~30 traders split 15/15, the 95% CI on the difference is about ±36 points (1.96 × √(0.25/15 × 2)). That
cannot tell "within 15 points" from ">30". T8 and T10 set no n.
Fix: make T4 qualitative or require ≥100 per arm. Give T8 and T10 a minimum n and CI rules.

**11. Exec #5, §8: polling stated as a current breach.**
Problem: nothing calls `/api/sync` yet, and the cron is owner-only (FC-K8). "No fresher data" is verified for
transactions, not for positions.
Fix: "As designed, it **would** exceed guidance once the cron is on. Transactions gain nothing; the gain for positions
is unclear."

**12. Exec #6, §8: vendor terms read more firmly than FC-M10 allows.**
Problem: FC-M10 leaves open whether a derived percentage is "Market Data". Read literally ("derived from data provided
by any third party", which includes prices), the definition would also reach closed-trade returns, so "closed trades
less" is not textual. §2(d) applies only if public display is "re-packaging to third parties". §4 (termination at any
time) means a written answer reduces dependence but does not "settle" it.
Fix: "The text *may* cover open-position storage, public display and the paid tier, and read literally, closed
returns too. Only the vendor can say. An answer lowers the risk but does not remove it."

**13. §8/§12: freeze vs deletion is presented as new.**
Problem: POLICY P6 already says "a record freeze never blocks deletion", and COMPLIANCE §1 agrees.
Fix: cite P6, and narrow the open point to the survivorship skew left by traders who delete.

**14. Exec #7, §4: the whole-record package is overclaimed as unclaimed.**
Problem: FC-M21 says the broker feed's profiles "can be shared off-platform", but §4 drops this. Forex verified-record
services (BT-C8) already show whole-account histories that cannot be edited trade by trade. "17-year gap" reads
backwards: the pattern exists but hasn't spread to equities. The claim of absence also rests only on what members
found.
Fix: "Among the patterns found, no US-equity product combines no per-trade hiding, losses ranked like gains,
intervals, lower-bound ranking, freeze and percent-only (Medium-Low)."

**15. Exec #8, §2.1: the evidence for "unflattering records" is transferred too far.**
Problem: the <1% figure is for day traders on one Asian exchange, 1992-2006 (FC-R19). The 56% antiskilled figure
measures finfluencers' *recommendations*, not their trades (FC-R21). The disposition effect (investors realise gains
sooner than losses) predicts that *closed*-trade win rates flatter, with losses sitting in open positions. A source
for it must be opened before use.
Fix: rate transfer Low and add this hypothesis. It raises expected card share rates and makes TR-4 open positions the
crux of trust.

**16. Exec #10, §6: "POLICY closes most paid routes" is overstated.**
Problem: POLICY bars paying traders (F5) and rewarded referrals. It does not bar paid ads or disclosed non-trader
creators. The constraint the synthesis misses is P6's "no third-party trackers": with no ad pixels, there is no
optimisation loop. P4 also requires 18+ targeting.
Fix: restate the rule accurately and add the P6 point.

**17. §6 funnel: the miss (20-50×) is assumption-heavy.**
Problem: it applies the news-link ratios (4.7-7.5×), not the corrected 4.0-6.8× (FC-R30), to an image-first strategy
the synthesis itself recommends. It counts one share per sharer per week, and ignores bio links, forum threads and
views from the founder's own earned media. Thirty-four chat posts at 150 views each would meet the gate. The
recruitment plan's cost also goes unstated: 75-160 contacts a week for one founder.
Fix: "~1.5-50× by mix (Low)". Add founder-originated views and the founder's hours.

**18. §3: the shown arithmetic doesn't reproduce the totals, and the surveys are mixed.**
Problem: "Trade 4+" shows 32.7M in every column, but the base and high totals use 33.1M and 33.7M, and an unshown 82%
self-directed base. "24-30%" combines the two surveys that §2.3 says not to mix.
Fix: show 32.7/33.1/33.7M and 75/82/90%, and label the 24-30% range "spans two surveys".

**19. P4 wording.**
Problem: "43% of those aged 18-34" (§2.1, #9) and "35% aged 18-29" (§2.3) name cohorts that, as written, include minors.
Fix: use "investors aged 18-34" and "adults 18-29".

**20. Proposal #12 conflicts with F3a.**
Problem: "The whole record" as a lead claim implies completeness, yet options, short sales, transfers and unconnected
brokers are excluded.
Fix: lead with "No per-trade hiding; losses shown and ranked like gains", plus the F3 exclusions link. Send it for F3a
review.

**21. Proposal #6/T8/G6: three conditions are missing.**
- (a) Waitlist emails are personal data, so P6 and P3 apply.
- (b) A launch email isn't on F3b's explicit allowed list. Ask the legal committee.
- (c) Random price arms show prices the owner hasn't set (F-3 2026-10-25/26). They must be honoured, or disclosed as a
  survey.

Fix: add (a)-(c) to #6, and flag (c) for the owner.

**22. Proposal #11: the preview creates trader-level selection.**
Problem: a trader who sees the record and then declines is cherry-picking by trader (F4's spirit; RT-C2). The preview
also overlaps P6's requirement to list what becomes public.
Fix: frame it as the P6 disclosure. Publish an aggregate "previewed, didn't publish" count on "How records are
calculated". The owner decides.

**23. Proposal #3: privacy details are missing.**
Problem: bot filtering and referrer capture tend to log IPs, user agents and query strings (P3, P6).
Fix: store only a referrer bucket and the `via` value. Discard the IP after bot checks. Never join these records to
user ids.

**24. §7 leaves out the data-access and privacy forces.**
Problem: the task asks about regulatory forces. Missing are REG-C8 (the broker data-security amendments, which push
brokers toward vetted API partners) and the state privacy laws treating financial data as sensitive, plus
GLBA/Safeguards. COMPLIANCE §1 lists both.
Fix: add one item on vendor-mediated access as a market structure, and one on consent and deletion as launch
conditions.

**25. §5 option 4 / #7: the trial evidence is unverified.**
Problem: the 7-day trial finding comes from an abstract that UV-P2 flagged for checking. Pass 3 did not verify it.
Fix: label it "abstract only, unverified".

**26. Source key, line 490: a research organisation is named.**
Problem: the domain is allowed. The name in prose is unnecessary under a strict reading of T1.
Fix: "a nonpartisan research center's fact sheets".

#### Optional suggestions

- Exec #9: "$9.99 is a ceiling" is OPINION, Low. The bracketing prices are unverified (FC-M28).
- §2.1 S2: "wins-only posting" is a critical description of an identifiable firm. Use FC-M21's neutral wording, and
  drop the exact customer count from anything that leaves the council (T4's spirit).
- §2.1: "10 bets" should be "10 trades". Wagering vocabulary clashes with F6.
- §6: say that the card images already carry the F3 disclaimer (`app/u/[handle]/record-card-image.tsx`), so
  image-first posting is compatible.
- §9 table: replace "Unmeasurable" with "not instrumented as built (days, T3)".
- §4: policy choices can be copied cheaply by firms without the conflicting revenue model. Name what makes them durable
  (accumulated frozen history, long-record rankings), or lower the confidence.

**Coverage vs the task:** all ten questions are addressed. The gaps are the regulatory data and privacy forces (24),
the follower-chooser segment's size, and test sample sizes (10).
