# Part C. Pass 4: chair's first synthesis (superseded by Part A)

### Market research council, pass 4: chair's synthesis

**Member:** chair. **Pass:** 4 (synthesis). **Date:** 2026-11-01.
**Inputs:** docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/BRIEF.md, 11 pass-1 files, 11 pass-2 files and 3 pass-3 fact-checks (these override earlier claims they
correct), plus DESIGN §1.2/§3.5 and F-3. I re-ran `docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/model.mjs` and added the arithmetic below. I opened no
new web sources. This is market research, not legal or investment advice, and it recommends no security or trade.
DESIGN's "interviews of ICP-S1" have no record and are treated as unverified.

**Labels.** **FACT** = a source opened by a member and, where marked, re-verified in pass 3. **ESTIMATE** = arithmetic
on facts and stated assumptions. **OPINION/HYPOTHESIS** = judgment. Confidence: High / Medium / Low.
**Source ids.** `[XX-Cn]` = claim n in p1-XX. `[XX-Sn]` = source n in that file. `[XX-P2]` = that member's pass-2
file. `[FC-L#]`/`[FC-K#]` = legal/code fact-check rows. `[FC-M#]` = market fact-check rows. `[FC-R#]` = research
fact-check rows. Member keys: DA data analyst, MS market sizing, PR pricing, UT user researcher (traders), UV user
researcher (viewers), DI distribution, RT red team, REG regulatory, BT competitive (broker-native, trackers, vendor),
CCS competitive (copy and signals), SF competitive (social and forums).

---

#### 1. Executive summary: the ten findings

1. **Break-even is a ratio, not a percentage.** Each payer nets about $8.80 a month, so it can carry at most about 8.5
   connected traders. The owner's "1.7-2.8%" is only the 1,000-viewer case; at scale the base floor is 1.18%.
   [DA-C1, DA-C2, re-run] (High)
2. **Both deciding inputs are unmeasured, and the dashboard's ranges are probably off.** Base conversion (1.5%) is likely
   too high for share-link viewers. Base traders per viewer (10%) may be far too high. [PR-C3, DA-C5, DA-C9] (Medium)
3. **One week-8 gate cannot be measured as built, and two are only proxies.** There is no referrer or view capture, D7
   covers signed-in viewers only, and share tracking has a dead column and an unrecorded button. [FC-K1..K6] (High)
4. **At n=50 the share gate is a coin flip.** A true 60% passes 56% of the time, and a true 55% passes 29%. The gates
   are correlated, and none tests viewers per trader or willingness to pay. [RT-C9, re-computed] (High)
5. **The hourly sync breaks vendor polling guidance for no fresher data:** up to 24× (activities) and 6× (positions) per
   account per day. [FC-M18, FC-K8] (High)
6. **The vendor terms (2026-05-22) are stricter than assumed.** §2(f) bans *storing* Market Data, and the repo stores
   open-position percentages. §2(d) needs consent from both parties, §2(b) bars selling data, and §7(g) deletion
   overrides "freeze". A written answer gates everything else. [FC-M8..M13, BT-P2] (High on text)
7. **"Verified" and "multi-broker" are no longer a wedge.** A commission-free app broker with 28.7M funded customers
   opened a free, *trader-selected* verified feed to all eligible US customers. What is defensible is the whole-record
   trust package on an open-web link. [FC-M19..M21, BT-C5, CCS-P2] (High on facts, Medium on implication)
8. **Most records will be unflattering, and the largest audiences have the most to lose.** Fewer than 1% of day traders
   are predictably profitable, and 56% of finfluencers are "antiskilled", with more followers. A trust-safe cohort
   probably misses the views gate. [FC-R19, FC-R21, DI-P2] (High on evidence, Medium on transfer)
9. **The paying job is narrow.** The record is free, so "verify before I pay" brings traffic, not revenue. Only repeat
   follower-choosers plausibly pay, the audience holds small portfolios, and $9.99 is a ceiling.
   [PR-C4, PR-P2, FC-R5] (Medium)
10. **Distribution must be founder-led and image-first.** Link posts get 1.1% of feed views and are 4-7× less visible.
    Paid acquisition returns $31 contribution per $150 at base, and POLICY closes most paid routes. On assumed rates the
    views gate misses by 3-50×. [FC-R29, FC-R30, DA-C10, DI-C4, SF-P2] (Medium; size Low)

---

#### 2. Segments and jobs to be done

##### 2.1 Traders who would publish

**FACT (High).**
- 97% of persistent futures day traders lost money, and 0.4% out-earned a bank teller (one emerging market) [FC-R18].
- Fewer than 1% of day traders predictably earn positive returns net of fees [FC-R19].
- The most active households earned 11.4% a year against the market's 17.9% [FC-R25].
- Of ~29,000 finfluencers, 28% were skilled, 16% unskilled and 56% antiskilled; the antiskilled have more followers [FC-R21].
- Options: 21% of investors have traded them, 43% of those aged 18-34. The sync skips options [FC-R16, UT-P2].

**OPINION (Medium).** Most records will sit in F-4's "still early" or "losses" tiers [UT-C1]. TR-1..TR-8 stop
cherry-picking of trades, but not of traders (who connects, and when) [RT-C2, UT-P2].

| Segment | Job | What serves it | What works against it |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 signals seller (DESIGN ICP-S1) | Prove I'm real so buyers pay | Verified label, full history, card | Record shows bad months; forward alerts barred (F3b); counsel topic still open; venues elsewhere *pay* [CCS-C10, FC-M25] |
| S2 proud hobbyist (ICP-S2) | Status | Chart, card, feed reach | Wins-only habit impossible; options missing; ranking floor of 10 bets; app broker's feed now offers wins-only posting for free [UT C8, CCS-P2] |
| S3 accountability/process trader (proposed) | A public record I can't edit | Freeze, no hiding, intervals | Small audiences, so few external views [UT-C8] |
| S4 long-history steady investor (proposed) | Show a calm long record | "Long record" tier | Trades rarely; the feed favours big moves |

**The realistic first 50 (OPINION, Low-Medium) [UT-C10, DI-P2, SF-P2].** In order: traders who already publish full
statements by hand; accountability traders; long-history investors; small transparency-led sellers (held until the
legal committee rules on "paid signal sellers as users"); S2 traders with a good run, recruited one to one. All adults
on supported brokers. DI revised its channel #1 the same way [DI-P2].

##### 2.2 What makes a record worth sharing, and what happens to mediocre ones

**HYPOTHESIS (Low) [UT-C9, UT-P2].** Shares follow the chart's shape and sign, not the interval. Losing or flat records
get shared, more rarely, as proof of honesty ("I show my losses"), as self-deprecating humour (entertainment motive
59% vs 18% [FC-R6]), or by accountability traders. UT's 55% share-rate estimate used an unverifiable "15% edge" figure
[FC-R20]. With "40% green at first sync by luck" (assumption) it is 62%, so recruiting mix and market conditions decide
the gate, not card design [CCS-P2]. Fears (dollar amounts, judgment on losses, method theft, link security) are
hypothesis-grade [UT-C6]. A per-trade "one honest trade" card is still an F-4 open question.

##### 2.3 Viewers who would read, and who would pay

**FACT (High) [FC-R1..R7].**
- 29% of investors rely on social media or boards for decisions (60% at 18-34); 26% act on finfluencers (61% at 18-34).
- These users consult 7.6 sources vs 4.0 and check backgrounds more (36% vs 14%), yet 68-69% of those targeted by fraud
  lost money (26-29% for non-users).
- Reliance falls with portfolio size: 48% under $50K, 20% at $500K+.
- A different 2025 survey gives 24% (35% aged 18-29); don't mix the two.
- Short video is second-tier: 40% of followers use it, 77% use long-form video [FC-R3, UV-P2].

**OPINION (Medium) [UV-C8, PR-P2].** The *casual scroller* (largest) will not pay. The *pre-purchase checker* gets the
full answer free, and the target is usually absent [UV-C9]: traffic, not revenue. The *follower-chooser* (repeat
viewer with a shortlist) is the only plausible buyer, likely annual; size unmeasured. Compare must work on a phone:
24% of adults 18-29 are smartphone-dependent [FC-R32].

---

#### 3. Market size ranges

| Layer | Low | Base | High | Basis | Label |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| US adults | 267M | 267M | 267M | Census: 61.2M = 18.0% → ≈340M; minus 73.1M under 18 [FC-R13] | ESTIMATE (High) |
| Hold non-retirement securities | 90.8M | 92M | 93.5M | 267M × 34% (NFCS state survey) / 35% (SHED) [FC-R11, FC-R12] | ESTIMATE (High) |
| Trade 4+ times a year | 32.7M | 32.7M | 32.7M | × 36% (investor survey; mixes two samples) [FC-R14] | ESTIMATE (Medium) |
| Monthly+ self-directed traders | 8.6M | 13.6M | 19.7M | × 75-90% self-directed × 35-65% trading ≥12/yr (*assumptions*) [MS-C4] | ESTIMATE (Low-Medium) |
| Reachable via the vendor's broker list | 6.0M | 11.6M | 18.1M | × assumed coverage 70/85/92%; 38 listed entries, ~15 distinct US brokers [MS-C6, FC-M14, FC-M15] | ESTIMATE (Low) |
| Public P&L posters (supply pool) | 20k | 75k | 250k | No sourced lower bound [MS-C9, UT-P2] | ASSUMPTION |
| Investor-viewers of social investing content | 21.8M | 24.5M | 27.2M | 90.8M × 24-30% [MS-P2] | ESTIMATE (Medium) |
| Monthly viewers of trading content | 20M | 30M | 45M | Frequency and non-investor shares assumed [MS-C11] | ESTIMATE (Low) |
| SAM, Record tools | $0.68B | $1.35B | $2.5B | Viewer × trader overlap 30/40/50% (assumed) × $112.9/yr [MS-C13] | ESTIMATE (Low) |

**What the business depends on (High) [MS-C15, MS-P2, DA-P2].** The ceiling is 3-4 orders of magnitude above any
plausible obtainable share and does not bind. The funnel does:
1. **Viewers per connected trader.** The plausible trader_share range runs from 0.2% to 15% (~70×), which moves
   break-even from 0.07% to 1.75%. No other input moves it as much.
2. **Conversion as a stock** (active payers ÷ monthly unique viewers) [DA-C5].
3. **Willingness to publish a whole record with losses**, not pool size.
4. **External views per trader**, which follow a heavy tail [DI-C3].
5. **Vendor coverage of creators' actual brokers**, plus exclusion of simulated accounts [FC-M16].

MS's obtainable-share table swings more than 10× on viewers per trader. 10,000 traders bring 100k viewers at the
dashboard ratio, or 1.4M-4.3M at the gate ratio. The table also repeated the stock/flow error, so it is indicative only
[DA-P2, MS-P2].

---

#### 4. Competitive pattern map (generic descriptions only)

| Pattern | Verified how | Who pays whom | Key facts | Threat to us |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Large social copy-trading broker | Its own ledger only | Platform pays traders up to 1.5%/yr of assets copying them | ~3,300 program **members** ≈ 0.1% of funded accounts (end 2024); the number paid is not disclosed. 4,750 members at end 2025. First paid tier needs $400K copied, ≈$6K/yr. Outcome-screened and exclusive [FC-M22..M25] | Low in the US: paying for copied assets fits poorly with US adviser rules; whether US residents can be paid is not stated [FC-M26, REG-P2] |
| Broker-native verified feed (commission-free app broker) | Own ledger plus identity check | Free | 28.7M funded customers (Sep 2026). Open to all eligible US customers. **Posting is selective.** Licensed professionals barred. "Quick trade from posts" pre-fills a trade in one tap. Open-web visibility not stated [FC-M19..M21] | High to the *verified label*. Low to the whole record. "Fast to build, slow to clear compliance" for public web [REG-P2] |
| Broker-native verified share links and forum layers (outside the US) | Own backend; white-label holdings layer | Free | Exist; scale unknown [BT-C3] | Medium. The white-label layer could spread across brokers |
| Linked-broker social investing apps | Broker linking, "verified" claimed | Viewer-paid, reportedly $8-$20/month | Price set **unverifiable** in pass 3 [FC-M28]. Several allow selective hiding [BT-C5] | Medium. They have the data pipes, not the trust rules |
| Trade journals | Broker sync (counts may include file import) | Trader pays, ~$20-$80/month | Exact price set unverifiable; the range is plausible [FC-M29] | Fastest serious *capable* entrant [BT-C6, CCS-P2] |
| Portfolio trackers; disclosure-mirroring apps | Broker sync; lagged public filings | ~$7.50-$16/month; ~$10/month ($89.99/yr unsourced) | Holdings or filings focus [BT-C7, FC-M27] | Low (different jobs) |
| Signal and strategy marketplaces; crypto copy | One account, read-only investor password (forex); hypothetical history (strategy) | Subscribers pay the seller; marketplace keeps ~20% (forex) or 13-30% (strategy); crypto profit share 10-50% | [CCS-C5, C7, C8] | Most *motive* to add "linked external account" badges [CCS-C13] |
| Paid author marketplaces on research sites | None | Readers pay authors | 150 authors, 11,000 subscribers, $5.2M/yr, which is **2017** data; premium $299/yr [FC-M30, FC-M31] | Competes for the same "pay for judgment" wallet |
| Microblogs, boards, creators | Screenshots | Ads and creator funds | Link posts are 1.1% of feed views; antiskilled finfluencers attract more followers [FC-R29, FC-R21] | Complement: bio link, reply, thread |
| Forex verified-record services | Read-only terminal password (since 2009) | Unknown | 2010 source [BT-C8] | Pattern is familiar; built to sell copying |

**Defensible (OPINION, Medium) [BT synthesis, CCS-P2]:** the *whole-record trust package*. That means no per-trade
hiding, full history "as the broker provides", open positions, losses ranked like gains, intervals and lower-bound
ranking, freeze on disconnect, percent-only, unpaid traders (US-compatible), adults only, and a signed-out open-web
link. These are policy choices that incumbents' revenue models resist, not technology [CCS-C13].

**Not defensible:** "verified" (free and selective at scale), "multi-broker" (table stakes), the share card. RT-P2's
warning stands: a gap open for 17 years in the closest analogue may mean low demand rather than opportunity.

---

#### 5. Willingness to pay for Record tools ($9.99 / $99.99)

**FACT.**
- North America app medians: $9.99 monthly, $39.99 yearly, all categories [FC-M34].
- After 12 months, 44.1% of yearly and 17.0% of monthly plans are still paying; lower-priced plans keep more [FC-M35, M36].
- Day-35 download-to-paid: 2.1% freemium, 10.7% hard paywall [FC-M32].
- 40% of North American plans sold are yearly [FC-M33]. Card fees are 7.1% on $9.99 [FC-M39].
- 70% of social-media-using investors *rely on* paid newsletters or resources (40% of all investors). This measures
  reliance, not payment [FC-R4].

**ESTIMATE: model results.** Base case, steady state [DA-C1, re-run]:

| Viewers/month | 1k | 10k | 100k | ∞ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Break-even conversion, conservative | 5.58% | 3.73% | 3.55% | 3.53% |
| Break-even conversion, base | 2.89% | 1.35% | 1.20% | 1.18% |
| Break-even conversion, strong | 1.78% | 0.45% | 0.31% | 0.30% |

Break-even at scale is set by: conv × $8.80 ≥ trader_share × $1 + $0.004. The chair turned that into minimum monthly
viewers per connected trader, by conversion:

| Conversion (stock) | Max trader_share | Min viewers per trader |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1% | 0.48% | 208 |
| 0.25% | 1.80% | 56 |
| 0.5% | 4.00% | 25 |
| 1.5% | 12.8% | 8 |

**Dashboard ratio vs kill-gate-implied ratio [DA-P2].**
- The gate's 5,000 views a week ≈ 21,667 a month. At 1-3 views per unique that is 144-433 viewers per trader
  (trader_share 0.23-0.69%). MS-P2 reads DI's funnel as 1.5-4.5%, a third view between the two.
- At the dashboard's 10%, no conversion in 0.1-0.5% ever breaks even, at either price.
- At the gate-mid ratio (0.46%), $9.99 breaks even at 0.25% from ~11k monthly uniques. Profit at gate traffic: −$148,
  −$4, +$234 at 0.1/0.25/0.5%.
- $4.99/$39.99 nets $4.03 and needs ~2.2× the conversion. At 0.1% it never breaks even, since hosting alone costs $4
  per payer.

**Stock vs flow (ESTIMATE, illustration).** The benchmarks are flows. The model's conversion is a stock:
stock = cohort conversion × (new ÷ monthly uniques) × paying life.
With 60% new viewers (assumption) and a 16.8-month life, 0.1% of new viewers paying gives a 1.0% stock, and 0.03% gives
0.3%. The council's prior for the stock among share-link viewers is 0.1-0.5% [PR-C3]. UV-P2 says 0.25-0.5%, with no
source for 0.1%. Confidence: Low.

**OPINION (Medium) [PR-C4, PR-C9, UV-P2].** Record tools is convenience over free data. Willingness to pay is likely
bimodal: near zero for most viewers, high for those who already pay for judgment. Linked-broker apps reportedly charge
viewers $8.99-$19.99 [BT-P2], but those prices are unverified [FC-M28].

**Packaging options within POLICY** (none gates the record, charges traders, sells visibility or pushes anything):
1. Keep $9.99/$99.99 as the ceiling and test one lower arm. It wins only if conversion rises more than 2.2×.
2. Annual-first presentation, perhaps a deeper discount ($99.99 is 16.6% off twelve months) [PR-C2].
3. Free limited compare (two traders, one saved view). Needs the legal committee [PR-C5].
4. A 7-day card-up-front trial or 14-day money-back arm. A field study found 7 days the best trial length but did not
   test "no trial" [UV-P2].
5. A "supporter" plan with no visible badge, or a club plan (legal review needed).

Excluded by rule: alerts (F3b), paid follow or watchlist limits, boosted profiles (F5), paid open positions or theses,
anything charged to traders. Auto-renewal: the click-to-cancel rule was vacated, but the FTC opened new rulemaking
(ANPRM 13 Mar 2026). ROSCA and state laws apply, so the annual plan needs a pre-renewal notice with no record field
(F3b allows it) [FC-L23, PR-C7].

---

#### 6. Distribution channels within POLICY

**FACT.**
- Link posts were 1.1% of US feed views on a large general network in Q2 2026 (1.3% in Q1) [FC-R29].
- On a microblog, posts linking to other external sites had median visibility ~6.8× and ~4.0× lower (7.5×/4.7× for news
  links). Observational data [FC-R30].
- "59% of shared links never clicked" is directional only (2015, news links, one platform) [FC-R28].
- The +246% broadcast effect ran *inside* the host network, so it does not carry over to off-platform links [SF-P2].

**ESTIMATE: funnel arithmetic (rates assumed) [DI-C4, DI-C5, SF-P2, SF-C11].**
- Views per post = audience × seen × click. Hobbyist with 1,000 followers: 2. Seller with 10,000: 20. A 500-member chat
  group at 30%: 150.
- 30 sharers × 25 views = 750 a week, 15% of the gate. With the measured link penalty (seen 1.3-2.1%), a 10k seller
  yields 3-4 views, and the shortfall becomes 20-50×.
- The gate means 100 views per trader a week, or 167 per sharer. The median sharer brings far fewer.
- Supply: 1,000 contacts × 20% reply × 40% sign up × 50% connect = 40. Plan on 600-1,300 contacts, or ~100 warm connect
  attempts [UT-C10].

**Measurement trap [DI-P2].** Phones share the card as an image plus URL. Apps often drop the link, so visits arrive as
"direct". A first-party `?via=` parameter avoids depending on Referer.

| Best (in POLICY) | Why |
|---|---|
| Founder-led one-to-one recruitment of transparency-led traders, asking for a bio link, a pinned post and a link in their own community | Persistent, highest views per share, fits their job [DI-C4, DI-P2] |
| Native card *image* with the link in a reply, the bio, chat or a forum thread | Images travel, links don't. Discussion boards reach 40% of adults 18-29 and are the #2 follower channel [SF-C11, SF-P2, FC-R31] |
| Earned media and education on reading records honestly ("what a range means", "why losses show") | Regulator-documented screenshot problem; no completeness or fraud-detection claims (F3a) [DI-C7] |

| Worst | Why |
|---|---|
| Paid performance ads | Fintech registration costs $17.96 (2021-22 data); contribution LTV/CAC 0.21 at base [FC-M40, DA-C10] |
| Paid creators or influencers | FTC disclosure and monitoring duties; FINRA entanglement for representatives; F5 closes traders [DI-C8, FC-L24] |
| Rewarded referrals | Barred for traders (F5/F6). A viewer reward is a material connection that needs disclosure [DI-C10] |

SEO is slow for week 8. The sitemap lists no profiles [FC-K11].

---

#### 7. Regulatory forces shaping the market (findings, not legal advice)

1. **Publisher's exclusion (472 U.S. 181)** protects impersonal, disinterested, regular publication and "factual
   information about past transactions" (pp. 206-210) [FC-L1..L5]. Quote the regularity passage in full [FC-L2].
   OPINION (Medium/Low): it plausibly covers finished records. It is weaker for live open positions, per-viewer ordering
   (F1a) and paid watch-style views, and it does not protect traders [RT-P2]. REG reads the fit more favourably [REG-C1].
2. **Compensation** is "any economic benefit" from anyone (IAC draft; IA-1092 unverifiable) [FC-L7]. F5 adds no
   compensation but removes none that traders already earn elsewhere. Stock Social's own $9.99 rests on the exclusion
   [RT-P2, refuting REG-C2].
3. **Enforcement targets the "successful trader" persona** [FC-L9..L11, L16, L17, L19..L21]:
   - 8 charged (7 posing as successful traders, ~$100M);
   - a jury verdict across >30 microcaps;
   - false >$1B AUM claims (actual ~1.4%/month);
   - a pool that pledged to "post all trades" and took ≥$10.1M.

   Real records rule out invented performance, not scalping on real positions. The evidence shows supply-side fraud,
   not willingness to pay [RT-P2].
4. **Finfluencer rules are open, not law.** IAC draft disclosure and database recommendations [FC-L8]. FINRA RN 26-14
   treats finfluencer involvement as a supervision risk [FC-L27]. FINRA's sweep produced 5 actions plus 3 related
   [FC-L25]. Do not cite ESMA's "transparency of previous recommendations" as an influencer proposal [FC-L35].
5. **Brokers are "entangled" with posts they arrange** [FC-L24]. Public broker feeds are costly to supervise, which
   favours an independent publisher [REG-P2]. An adviser posting a card likely needs total-portfolio context
   [FC-L15, REG-P2].
6. **No federal right to brokerage data** (CFPB 1033 excludes investment accounts and is stayed), so vendor price is
   pure contract [FC-L31].
7. **Age assurance.** No. 23-1122 is limited to content obscene to minors. In No. 25A97 a concurrence notes seven
   district courts enjoined similar laws. "Easier to sustain" is unsupported [FC-L33, L34]. A watch item only.
8. **UK/EU.** FSMA s21 is criminal and cross-border [FC-L38]. EU regulators say a disclaimer "will not protect you"
   [FC-L36].
9. **FTC endorsement and review rules** make paid promotion costly. Unrewarded cards need no disclosure
   [REG-C11, FC-L22].

---

#### 8. Platform risk (SnapTrade)

**FACT (High). Developer Terms, "Last Updated: May 22, 2026"** [FC-M7]. BT's 2024 date is stale.

| Clause | Text | Hits |
|---|---|---|
| §2(b) | No "sell, license, or purchase any data obtained from SnapTrade" [FC-M8] | The paid tier [BT-P2] |
| §2(d) | No re-packaging to third parties without written consent of **both** End User and SnapTrade [FC-M9] | Public profiles; the trader's opt-in is half |
| §2(f) | "will not **store**, resell, or redistribute Market Data" (includes third-party prices) [FC-M10] | Stored `unrealizedPct`, computed from "Last known market price" [FC-K9]; open positions (TR-4, F-6) carry the most risk, closed trades less [BT-P2] |
| §7(a) | Market data only "to serve the End User" [FC-M11] | Public open-position values |
| §7(g) | Delete all End User data on request [FC-M12] | Caps TR-5's freeze; reopens selection [REG-P2] |
| §4, §12 | Termination "at any time"; 30 days' price notice; broker list changes without notice [FC-M13] | Single-vendor dependence |

**Pricing (FACT).**
- $1/connected user/month on the daily plan, $2 real-time, plus $100/month. The terms elsewhere say "no monthly minimum";
  ask [FC-M1, M2].
- Billing continues until **every** connection is deleted, so frozen-but-connected records cost $1/month forever [FC-M4].
- The $0 OAuth offer likely does not apply [FC-M5].
- A processing fee of up to 3% may be added [BT-P2].
- $0.50 is unsupported [FC-M6]. Manual sync is $0.05, but the code never calls it [FC-M3].

**Polling (FACT, High).**
- Guidance: activities at most once per account per 24h, positions at most 4 times a day. Transactions are cached,
  refreshed daily and delayed a day on both plans [FC-M18].
- The code allows a claim every hourly round with both calls: 24× and 6× the guidance [FC-K8].
- Mitigation: nothing calls `/api/sync` yet [FC-K8].

**Coverage.**
- Say "38 listed" (F3a) [FC-M14, M17].
- 3 simulated brokerages exist. The code drops `is_paper` accounts, but whether the vendor sets that flag is unverified
  [FC-M16, FC-K7].
- Crypto and forex are barely covered [BT-P2].

**OPINION (chair).** On the text, BT is right over RT ("conflict") and REG ("ambiguous"). On their face the clauses
cover today's design. Only the vendor's reading is unknown, and one written exchange settles it.

---

#### 9. Week-8 kill gates: are they set right?

**Measurability in the code as built (FACT, High).**

| Gate | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| ≥50 connected traders | Measurable | Count non-demo users with a live connection. Paper accounts are excluded [FC-K7] |
| ≥60% share at least once | Proxy only | `share_prompt_action`, `share_image_saved_at`, `trade_shared_at` are live. **`badge_copied_at` is dead.** The month-recap share is never recorded. Cancelled sheets aren't counted, and failed shares count as "copied". A copy is not a post [FC-K4..K6] |
| ≥5,000 external-referrer views/week | **Unmeasurable** | No Referer handling and no view table among 21 tables. `landed` fires only when signup starts [FC-K1, FC-K2] |
| Viewer D7 ≥15% | Rough, signed-in only | `last_seen_at` is overwritten at most hourly. Signed-out viewers leave no trace [FC-K3] |
| (supporting) consent decline reasons | Not capturable | The `declined` event takes no reason [FC-K10] |

**Statistical power (ESTIMATE, High).**

| True share rate | 50% | 55% | 60% | 65% | 70% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P(pass ≥30/50) | 10% | 29% | 56% | 81% | 95% |

D7 at 15% has a 95% half-width of ±7.0 points at n=100 and ±4.9 at n=200. The gate sets no minimum cohort.

**Other weaknesses.**
- **Correlated gates.** Views and D7 both ride on share links, because F3b removes push [UV-P2]. Sharing and views both
  depend on cohort mix [DI-P2]. "Miss two or more" can therefore fail as one event, or pass on a warm network.
- **Crawlers.** Link-preview crawlers inflate views unless bots are filtered [RT-C9].
- **Missing gates.** Viewers per trader and payment intent are the two inputs that decide survival [RT-C9, DA-C9].
- **Weak stop action.** "Re-interview ICP-S1" has no baseline to compare against.

**Proposed revised gates (PROPOSALS for the owner; DESIGN §3.5 is the owner's).** Definitions should be pre-registered
and committed before week 1.

| # | Metric (definition) | Continue | Inconclusive → extend 4 weeks | Stop signal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | Connected adult traders, non-demo, non-paper, ≥1 imported closed trade | ≥50 | 35-49 | <35 |
| G2 | Distinct G1 traders with a completed share or copy event (recap recorded; dead badge column excluded); report copy and share separately, with 95% CI | ≥33/50 (66%) | 26-32 | ≤25 (≤50%) |
| G3 | Monthly unique external viewers per connected trader (first-party, cookie-free, bots filtered, referrer bucket + `?via=`) | ≥50 | 25-49 | <25 (fails even at 0.5% conversion) |
| G4 | Absolute external uniques per week (same instrument) | ≥2,500 | 1,000-2,499 | <1,000 |
| G5 | D7 among viewers who signed in or followed, cohort n≥200 | ≥15% | 10-15% | <10% |
| G6 | Payment intent: painted-door waitlist joins at a shown price, % of signed-in returning viewers | ≥3% | 1.5-3% | <1.5% |
| G0 | Vendor written answer on §2(b), §2(d), §2(f), §7(a), §7(g) | Received, permissive | n/a | Refusal → redesign before launch |

**Proposed decision rule.**
- G0 and G1 are hard gates.
- G3 × G6 is the economic test. Continue only if implied conversion clears the G3 threshold table in §5.
- G2, G4 and G5 are diagnostic: two stop signals among them trigger the re-interview.

G4 halves the absolute views target because G3 carries the economics. That change is a proposal and contested (§12).

---

#### 10. The projection dashboard: verdict and corrections

**"Break-even at roughly 1.7-2.8% of monthly viewers paying": partly right, misleading as a headline (High).**
- It covers the strong and base cases at 1,000 viewers (1.78%, 2.89%). It omits the conservative case: 5.58%, and
  never at its own conversion.
- At scale the base floor is 1.18%, and 0.30-3.53% across scenarios [DA-C1]. F-3's 2026-10-25 rationale quotes the 1k
  case.
- Better headline: "**each payer covers broker cost for at most ~8.5 connected traders at $1 (~4 at $4.99)**"
  [DA-C2, PR-P2].

**"Broker data about $1 per connected trader a month": price verified, cost basis incomplete (High)** [FC-M1..M6].
- Add billing until the last connection is deleted, and a possible 3% fee.
- Use $2 as the stress case, since prices can change on 30 days' notice.
- Drop $0.50, and never use $0 OAuth.

| Input | Problem | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| `conv` | Benchmarks are flows with committed denominators; "1-3% of free readers" unsourced [DA-C5, FC-M32, M41] | Define as a stock = flow × new share × paying life; prior 0.1-0.5%; add conv-by-price |
| `price` | No scenario variation, so the tornado shows zero [PR-P2] | Add a price input (break-even ∝ 1/net revenue) |
| `trader_share` | 5-15% assumed [DA-C9] | Add gate (0.2-0.7%) and DI-funnel (1.5-4.5%) scenarios; plot results on this axis |
| `churn_monthly` | The 3.6% "median a month" is **annual** in its source [FC-M38] | Drop it. App data imply ~13.7%/month blended, front-loaded [DA-C6, MS-P2]. 7% is optimistic; 10% suggested [PR-P2] |
| `annual_share` | Global 34% | North America 40% [FC-M33]; immaterial |
| `cac_paying` | $11/$18 are **cost per registration**, 2021-22 mobile data [FC-M40] | Relabel. $150 = $10.87 ÷ ~7% is a stale estimate |
| b03, b06, b10, b20, b27 | Program members (not paid traders); $89.99 unsourced; 2017; annual; registration | Fix the text [FC-M note 5] |

**Measure first:** monthly unique external viewers per connected trader, then payers per trader once billing runs
[DA, RT-C5]. This needs the G3 instrument, which does not exist yet.

#### 11. Cheapest tests first

Ordered by cost. Thresholds are proposals.

| # | Test | Cost | Unlocks | Pass / fail |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Written questions to SnapTrade (proposal #1) | ~$0 | Launch as designed? | Pass: permission for public closed %, stored/public open %, paid tier. A refusal on open positions means redesign (TR-4/F-6, owner) |
| T2 | Sync cadence fix | Hours | Vendor compliance | Logs show ≤1 activities and ≤4 positions calls per account-day |
| T3 | Measurement build (proposal #3) | Days | Reading G2-G5 | Definitions committed before week 1; synthetic visits counted |
| T4 | Whole-record preview, ~30 traders | Low | Viable honest supply? [RT-C1] | Pass: unclear/negative records publish within 15 points of positive ones. Fail: gap >30 |
| T5 | Outreach to 100 transparency-led traders | Founder time | First-50 mix | ≥20% connect [RT-C3 bar]. Seller arm waits for the legal committee |
| T6 | 10-15 interviews per segment | Low | Re-interview baseline | Qualitative |
| T7 | G3 viewers per trader, weekly | $0 after T3 | Unit economics | ≥50; <25 is a stop signal |
| T8 | Painted door ("isn't available yet; no charge"), random price arms, annual shown | Low | WTP and price | ≥3% of signed-in returning viewers join the waitlist [PR] |
| T9 | Price survey, ≥200 per segment | Low | Acceptable range | Price inside the range; stated values are upper bounds |
| T10 | Adult comprehension of the interval line | Low | F-4 copy | ≥80% read it as past results, not a forecast |
| T11 | After P6 and T1: real-money launch, no trial vs trial or money-back | Medium | Real stock conversion | At or above the §5 threshold for the measured G3 ratio |

#### 12. Dissent and unresolved disagreements

- **Traders per viewer.** Owner: 5-15%. DA: 0.2-0.7% from the gates. MS-P2: 1.5-4.5% (DI funnel), and calls DA's
  inference circular. Measure it (T7).
- **Conversion.** Dashboard: 0.5/1.5/3%. PR: 0.1-0.5% stock. UV-P2: 0.25-0.5%. DA: no value derivable.
- **ICP-S1.**
  - DI-C4: recruit large sellers to reach the views gate (DI later revised this).
  - SF-P2, UV-C9, RT-C3: large sellers have the least incentive and the most legal exposure.
  - UT-P2: small transparency sellers are viable.
  - SF-P2: the views gate is unlikely to be met under any mix.
- **Views gate.** DI: misses by 3-10×. SF-P2: by 20-50×. RT would make gates stricter, not halve G4.
- **Fast follower.** BT: journals and linked apps, which have the capability. CCS: marketplaces, which have the motive.
  REG-P2: broker feeds, fast to build but slow to clear compliance.
- **Publisher's exclusion.** REG: Stock Social fits the protected side. RT-P2: the fit is weaker for open positions, F1a
  ordering and paid views. Open until the legal committee rules.
- **Freeze vs deletion.** BT-P2: deletion weakens "freeze deters connects". REG-P2: deletion reopens selection. Both hold.
- **Willingness to pay.** PR: $3-6. UV-P2: bimodal. BT-P2: viewer-paid apps exist, prices unverified.
- **Churn.** DA: 13.7%. MS-P2 and PR-P2: front-loaded, so 10% base. Affects LTV only.
- **Demand from enforcement.** REG-C3: proves demand. RT-P2: shows supply-side fraud only.
- **Market timing.** RT-C10: the cycle is fading (new investors 21%→8%; non-retirement investing among those aged 18-34 32%→26%,
  corrected [FC-R9, FC-R10]). Uncontested.
- **Resolved by fact-check or concession.**
  - F5 and compensation: RT-P2 over REG-C2.
  - Vendor terms: BT over RT and REG.
  - The $4.99 arm: PR accepted DA-P2.
  - Age-assurance trend: FC-L34 corrects REG-C9.

# Part D. Pass 3: fact-checks

## D1. Research figures (surveys, statistics, academic studies)

### Pass 3 fact-check: research domain (surveys, statistics, academic figures)

Member: fact-checker (research). Pass 3, 2026-11-01. Scope: survey, statistics and academic figures that p1/p2 conclusions rest on.
Method: I re-opened each primary source with WebFetch. For PDFs, I saved the file and extracted its text with pdftotext, then read the
passage myself. Third-party platform names appear in some sources; per T1 they are described generically below.
Verdicts: VERIFIED = source says this; CORRECTED = right figure given; UNVERIFIABLE = could not open a primary; FALSE = source contradicts.

| R28 | 59% of shared URLs never clicked (DI C3) | VERIFIED (secondary only) / primary UNVERIFIABLE | The primary conference paper (a computer-systems measurement conference, 2016) is hosted on an open archive (403/anti-bot) and a university mirror (403); neither opened. A university-system press release says "59 percent of all links shared in their sample went unclicked". The sample: about 1% of public posts on a microblog, shortened links to 5 news outlets, one month in summer 2015, about 10M clicks. The stats blog DI used says the 59% is for links shared by others, not the outlet. That matches DI's wording; the release's "all links" is looser. Use as directional only: 2015 data, news links, one platform. | F17, F18 |
| R29 | 1.1% of US feed content views were posts with an external link in Q2 2026, down from 1.3% in Q1 (CSF B-C9) | VERIFIED | The large general social network's own transparency report (widely viewed content report), Q2 2026 (1 Apr-30 Jun): "For the 1.1% of views in posts that did include a link…"; the Q1 2026 report gives 1.3%. US viewers only (companion guide). | F19 |
| R30 | Link posts' median visibility 7.5× and 4.7× lower; 4.3M and 34.8M posts (CSF B-C9; DI said "not in abstract") | VERIFIED (full text) | arXiv 2410.17390 v4 (8 Sep 2025; NDSS 2026), §5.1 and Table 1. The visibility measure is views ÷ author followers. Medians: no link 0.246 vs news-outlet link 0.033 (≈7.5×, 4,306,644 posts, war topic); 0.056 vs 0.012 (≈4.7×, 34,762,696 posts, election topic). **The ratios are against news-outlet links specifically.** Against other external websites they are ≈6.8× and ≈4.0×. The abstract says "up to a factor of eight". The ratios are computed from medians, the data are observational, and they cover two political topics. | F20 |
| R31 | National research organization: adults 18-29 use long-form video 95%, photo 79%, short video 65%, microblog 31%, discussion board 40%; n=5,511, Jan 30-Jun 18 2026 (CSF, DI, MS S7) | VERIFIED | National research organization social media fact sheet, 19 Oct 2026. All adults: long-form video 84%, general network 70%, photo 51%, short video 31%, microblog 25%, discussion board 18%. 18-29 general network 69%. MOE ±1.8. | F21 |
| R32 | 24% of adults 18-29 are smartphone-dependent (UV item 7) | VERIFIED | National research organization mobile fact sheet, 19 Oct 2026, same survey: 18-29 at 24%; all adults 16%. | F22 |
| R33 | 37% of adults 18-29 regularly get news from news influencers vs 21% of all adults (UV C1) | VERIFIED | National research organization journalism report, 18 Nov 2024; >10,000 adults, survey Jul-Aug 2024. These are news influencers, not finance ones. | F23 |
| R34 | D7: about 20% for finance apps, 10.9% across apps (DI C12) | VERIFIED as quoted, but the source is unfit | An app-tooling vendor's 2026 benchmarks blog gives an all-app Day 7 of 10.9% (11.5% in 2024) and an "average" of 20% for finance/fintech. It states no data source, sample or method. Other vendor blogs put finance D7 at about 18-18.5% and cross-industry at 11-13%. No primary (analytics-provider dataset) was opened. All of these are installed-app cohorts; none covers web visitors from a shared link. Do not use any of them as the basis for the ≥15% gate. | F24 |

Count: 34 items.

#### Notes

1. **Survey bases get mixed.** The NFCS has two linked samples: State-by-State (25,539 adults, which gives 34%) and the Investor Survey
   (2,861 non-retirement investors, which gives 36%, 29%, 26%, 21% and 43%). The Foundation brief's percentages are of investors, not of
   adults. The regulator report's 24%/35% comes from a third, 2025 survey. Any funnel that multiplies these should label each base.
2. **R10 is the one outright conflict between sources.** The full report (32%→26% for investors aged 18-34) outranks the press release
   (26%→21%). RT and UT should restate the figure.
3. **R4 is a reliance measure, not payment.** "Rely a great deal or somewhat on paid newsletters or online resources" (70%) is not "pays
   for a subscription". The all-investor baseline is 40%. It supports "this audience already uses paid sources". It does not support
   any conversion rate.
4. **R30 sharpens the link penalty.** The 7.5×/4.7× ratios are for news-outlet links. For other external sites (a share card would be one)
   the paper's medians give about 6.8× and 4.0×. The direction and rough size stand. DI was right that the medians are not in the
   abstract, but they are in the full text.
5. **Research findings that bear on design are confirmed** (R22-R24): social exposure → more disposition effect and risk-taking, with copying
   worse still; attention-driven buying → −4.7% over 20 days. This supports the no-alerts, no-copy and losses-ranked-equally rules.
   R22 is convergence of behavior, not trade herding.
6. **Not checked (outside this domain):** vendor pricing, regulatory texts, app-store and subscription-report figures, and
   competitor company figures.

#### Open questions
- Which measure is the release's 21% (R10)? Ask the Foundation, or find the release's footnote.
- Is there a primary D7 benchmark for finance apps, ideally for web visitors from shared links? None found. Measure our own.
- The 59% primary paper: get the full text through an institutional route if the figure is to stay in any arithmetic.

## D2. Market figures (vendor, competitor and pricing)

### P3 fact-check: market (vendor, competitor and pricing figures)

Member: fact-checker, domain "market". Pass 3, 2026-11-01. Every source below was re-opened by me today (WebFetch, curl, or
SEC EDGAR). Repo claims were checked read-only against the repository. Third-party companies are described
generically (POLICY T1/T2). URLs are given only where the domain and path carry no third-party brand.

Verdicts: VERIFIED (the source says exactly this) / CORRECTED (right figure and source given) / UNVERIFIABLE (what I
tried is stated) / FALSE.

#### A. SnapTrade pricing, terms, coverage and polling

| # | Claim (files) | Verdict | What the source says | Source, location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | $1 per connected user/month (daily), $2 (real-time) (DA C4, b22, UT, REG, RT) | VERIFIED | "Daily: $1 per connected user / month"; "Real-time: $2 per connected user / month" (app-managed users) | [V1] pricing page, plan table |
| 2 | $100/month platform fee | VERIFIED, with a caveat | Pricing page: Launch "$100 / month + per-user pricing", and the fee "still applies" to Personal/OAuth users. **Caveat:** the Developer Terms (Introduction/Service, plan definitions) describe the Pay-as-You-Go Real-time plan as having "no monthly minimum". The page and the terms disagree; the page is the operative price list ("Pricing is set forth on .../pricing"). Ask in writing. | [V1]; [V2] "Service" section |
| 3 | $0.05 per manual sync | VERIFIED | "$0.05 per connection per sync", Daily plan only; included on Real-time. Repo: `lib/broker.ts` makes no refresh/manual-sync call, so current code incurs no such charge. | [V1] FAQ; repo grep |
| 4 | Billing per user, not per connection; "linked at least one account" (UT item 1) | VERIFIED | A connected user is "a user who has successfully connected at least one brokerage account"; one user with several connections counts once. **New:** the terms say that to avoid billing for a user next month, the client "must delete the applicable connection(s) before the end of the current billing period". A record kept "frozen" while its connection stays open keeps costing $1/month. | [V1] FAQ; [V2] Fees, item ii |
| 5 | "Personal/OAuth users are free for a limited time"; end date? | VERIFIED (no end date exists) | The page gives no end date or duration; after it ends they pay the app's Daily/Real-time rate. Inference (High): Stock Social registers users through the SDK, so its users are app-managed ("Commercial" in [V4]), and the offer doesn't apply. The $0 case should not appear in any projection. | [V1]; [V4] "Two customer models" |
| 6 | Volume discounts on a custom plan only; the $0.50 "strong" value | VERIFIED (discounts) / the $0.50 stays unsupported | "Volume discounts as you scale" on Custom only; no published rate. | [V1] |

| 9 | §2 "re-package" with written consent of both | VERIFIED | (d) "Not to mine, re-sell, or re-package any End User data obtained via the SnapTrade API to third parties without the express written consent of both the End User and SnapTrade." | [V2] Limitations on Use (d) |

"Market Data" means data obtained through the API "in connection with End User data, which data is licensed, made available by or derived from data provided by any third party" (includes prices, security descriptions). The ban covers *storing*, not only display. Repo: `lib/sync.ts#replacePositions` stores `unrealizedPct` per open position. Whether a derived percentage counts as "derived from" Market Data is a question for the vendor. | [V2] (f) and Definitions |

| 14 | Brokerage list: 38 enabled entries, all read-only capable (MS C5) | VERIFIED | Public API returns 38 entries, all `enabled: true`, all list `read` in `authorization_types`, all `GENERALLY_AVAILABLE`. **New:** one Canadian entry had `maintenance_mode: true` today. | [V3] |
| 15 | ~16 US stock/ETF entries; 6 CA, 2 AU, 2 IN, 2 UK, 2 EU, 3 crypto, 1 retirement recordkeeper, 3 paper (MS C5) | VERIFIED (estimate holds) | My own classification: 16 US self-directed entries, but one large US broker appears **twice** (two connection methods), so about 15 distinct US brokers; plus 1 multi-country copy-trading broker. 3 entries typed "Cryptocurrency Exchange". The other counts match. | [V3] |
| 16 | 3 paper/simulated entries; does the vendor set `is_paper` on them? (MS C7, DA, UT) | PARTLY VERIFIED / UNVERIFIABLE | The 3 entries exist (slugs end in SIM, PAPER, PRACTICE). The API reference defines a required account field `is_paper`, "whether the account is a paper (simulated) trading account". No public doc says the vendor sets it for these 3 brokerages. Repo: `lib/broker.ts` maps `is_paper`, and `lib/sync.ts` skips paper accounts (lines 183, 316) and excludes them from rebuilt trades (88, 112). So MS C7's "simulated trades would display as Verified" is **out of date** *if* the flag is set. Backstop: exclude the 3 brokerage slugs too. | [V3]; [V5] list-accounts schema; repo |
| 17 | Coverage "450M+ accounts across 40+ institutions" (BT C10) | VERIFIED (docs) | Docs: "450M+ retail investor accounts across 40+ financial institutions". Other vendor pages say 400M+/35+. The public list has 38 entries. Use "38 listed" in product copy (POLICY F2: no completeness claims). | [V6] Core Concepts page |
| 18 | Activities no more than once per account per 24h; balances/positions/orders ≤4×/day in background (BT C12) | VERIFIED, and the code conflicts | Checklist item 2: "You don't call the activities (transactions) endpoint more than once per account every 24 hours"; "shouldn't call the Balances, Positions and Orders endpoint more than 4 times per day per user in the background". Also, "Transactions are cached, updated once per day, and delayed by one day on both Real-time and Daily plans." Repo: `lib/sync.ts` claims every syncable trader once per hourly round (ROUND_MS = 3,600,000) and calls `fetchActivities` and `fetchPositions` on each sync. That is up to 24 activities calls and 24 positions calls per account per day: 24× and 6× the guidance, for no fresher data. | [V7] launch guide, item 2; [V8]; repo `lib/sync.ts` 140-200, 300-350 |

#### Short notes

1. **Hourly sync against vendor guidance (#18)** is the most actionable finding. The fix is a code change for the
   engineer, not a pricing change: sync activities once per account per 24h, spread across the day; positions ≤4×/day.
   No data is lost because transactions refresh daily anyway.
2. **The terms are stricter than the council quoted (#10).** §2(f) bans *storing* Market Data. Together with §2(d) (written consent
   from both End User and SnapTrade to re-package to third parties) and §7(a), this makes the vendor's written answer a
   pre-launch gate (RT C8 stands). Ask about: (a) public display of derived percentages, (b) stored open-position
   percentages, (c) the $100 fee vs "no monthly minimum".
3. **Billing continues on frozen records (#4).** TR-5 ("records freeze on disconnect") is safe only while the vendor
   connection is deleted. A record that is "frozen" but still connected costs $1/month indefinitely.
4. **Competitor base:** cite 28.7M (Sept 2026) for the app broker. For the copy broker, "~3,300 program members (0.1%),
   number paid not disclosed; first paid tier $400K AUC → ≈$6K/yr". That strengthens CCS's "tiny tail" conclusion.
5. **Benchmarks to fix in the dashboard's benchmark list:** b03 (members, not paid; primary source is the 424B4), b06
   (drop $89.99), b10 (2017, different source), b20 (annual label, not monthly), b27 (registration, not install).
6. The model was not re-run here (outside the market domain). None of the corrections changes the $1 + $100 vendor inputs.
   The churn and CAC corrections touch the bases only, not the values.

## D3. Legal citations and code claims

### Pass 3: Fact-check, domain "legal-and-code"

**Member:** fact-checker (legal/regulatory sources + repo code claims). **Pass:** 3, 2026-11-01.
**Method:** every legal item was re-opened at the primary source (PDFs downloaded and text-extracted where WebFetch could not parse them;
opinions read at the page cited). Code items were read at repo commit 15ca6c3, read-only. Not legal advice. Cases are cited by reporter,
docket or release number only (POLICY T1/T2); parties are not named here.

Verdicts: **V** = VERIFIED, **C** = CORRECTED, **U** = UNVERIFIABLE, **F** = FALSE.

# Part E. Pass 2: cross-examinations

## E1. Market sizing analyst (challenges: data analyst, distribution)

### Market sizing: Pass 2 (cross-examination)

**Member:** Market Sizing Analyst. **Pass:** 2. **Date:** 2026-11-01.
**Targets:** p1-data-analyst.md (DA) and p1-distribution.md (DI). **Own file:** p1-market-sizing.md (MS).
**Fetches this pass (4):** the data vendor's pricing page (SnapTrade, [S1] in DA), FINRA's Apr 2026 release (DI [S5]),
FINRA's Dec 2025 social-media report (DI [S6]), and a university statistics blog (DI [S4]). No company is named.

---

#### A. Data analyst (p1-data-analyst.md)

**DA-C1. The owner's 1.7–2.8% break-even holds only at about 1k viewers; at scale it drops to about 1.2% (base).**
I reproduced the variable-only floor independently (MS C14: 0.104 / 8.80 ≈ 1.18%), so the arithmetic matches. My pass-1
line, "consistent with the owner's 1.7–2.8% once fixed costs are added", was too loose. Fixed costs only explain that
range at small scale. DA's restatement is the better one. **Verdict: STANDS.** In the conservative case, break-even is
never reached (3.5% needed against 0.5% converting), so a single headline range hides the case where the business fails.

**DA-C2. The binding condition is ≤ ~8.5 connected traders per paying viewer at $1 per trader.**
Check: conv × 8.80 ≥ trader_share × 1 + 0.004. With hosting per payer 0.004/0.015 = 0.27, that gives
trader_share/conv ≤ 8.53. **Verdict: STANDS.** This framing is better than the TAM framing and supersedes MS C15 #1,
where I ranked viewers per trader and conversion as two separate items. In fact they form one ratio.

**DA-C4. Broker cost $1/user/month; $0.50 strong case unsupported; personal/OAuth users "$0 for a limited time".**
Confirmed on the pricing page this pass:
- $100 platform fee; $1 daily / $2 real-time per connected user; $0.05 per manual sync on daily.
- Multiple brokerage connections count as one user.
- "Personal/OAuth users are free for a limited time", with no duration given. The $100 fee still applies.
- Volume discounts appear only on the custom plan, with no rates published.

**Verdict: STANDS.** One addition from the sizing side: if most supported US brokers connect through OAuth, early broker
cost may be close to $0. But "limited time" with no end date means the model should not count on it. Keep $1 as the base.

**DA-C5. Conversion benchmarks use committed-user denominators (installs, email sign-ups), and the model's conv is a
stock while the benchmarks are flows.**
**Verdict: STANDS.** It also exposes an error in my own file: MS C14's SOM table applies 0.5/1.5/3% to monthly viewers as
a stock, which is the same mistake. Revised MS claim: the SOM payer counts are indicative only, and could be off by a
multiple of 2–5x in either direction until the cohort (flow) conversion and payer life are modelled. Payers ≈ monthly
new-viewer flow × cohort conversion × expected paying life.

**DA-C6. 17% of monthly plans retained at 1 year implies ~13.7%/month churn.**
1 − 0.17^(1/12) = 13.7% is correct as arithmetic. But app subscription churn is front-loaded: most loss happens in months
1–3. The geometric mean therefore overstates steady-state churn among surviving payers and understates early churn.
It is a fair blended figure for LTV, but not for "monthly churn". **Verdict: WEAKENED.** Revised claim: blended churn over
the first year is ~13.7%/month for app-store monthly plans. Steady-state churn is lower and is not measured by this source.
This web product has no app-store data of its own.

**DA-C9. The kill gates imply trader_share of 0.2–0.7%, far below the owner's 5–15%.**
This is circular: it infers a cost input from a design target. DI's own funnel (DI C4) estimates that a hobbyist-heavy
cohort of 50 produces ~750 views/week, about 3.2k/month. At 1–3 views per unique viewer, that is ~1.1k–3.2k uniques, so
trader_share ≈ 1.5–4.5%, which falls between the two. **Verdict: WEAKENED.**
- Revised: early trader_share is unknown within roughly 0.2%–15%, about a 70x band. Two council members disagree on it
  using only assumptions.
- Implication for MS C14: the viewer column could be 10–30x larger or smaller. The high case's 1M viewers on 50k traders
  is not a ceiling.
- Measuring trader_share is the right first step, but it requires the first-party view counting that DI C1 says does not
  exist yet.

**DA-C10. Paid acquisition does not pay at base (contribution LTV $31 vs CAC $150).** The CAC source was not opened and is
dated, so the conclusion rests partly on an unverified input. **Verdict: STANDS (direction), NEEDS FACT-CHECK (CAC).**
It agrees with DI's "worst channel #1". With no free trial, there is also no cheap step at which paid traffic can be
qualified before it costs money.

#### B. Distribution (p1-distribution.md)

**DI-C1. Three of four week-8 gates cannot be measured as built.**
This is a code claim I did not re-check. It is also the most decision-relevant claim across all three files: the DA's
"measure trader_share first" and my C15 #4 both depend on it. **Verdict: STANDS pending an engineer check.**

**DI-C3. 59% of shared links are never clicked.**
Opened [S4]. The figure refers to shortened news links shared on one microblog by accounts other than the publisher.
The blog itself warns against reading it as being about readers. It is old (paper reported 2016), comes from a single
platform, and concerns news rather than finance or image unfurls. **Verdict: WEAKENED** as a quantitative input. As a
qualitative point, that a heavy tail is likely, it stands. A better phrasing: expect most shares to drive near-zero views;
the 59% figure is illustrative only.

**DI-C4. The 5,000-views gate needs about a quarter of the 50 traders to be paid-group sellers with 10k+ audiences.**
The arithmetic is internally consistent, but every rate is an assumption. The supply side is the weak point:
- My C9 estimates only ~1k–25k US paid-group or signal sellers.
- The academic study in MS [S8] found that the most-followed posters were more often "antiskilled", i.e. the people with
  the most to lose from a full record that includes losses.
- So the 12–13 large-audience sellers the gate needs are the creators least likely to connect.

**Verdict: WEAKENED.** Revised claim: the views gate depends on recruiting the creators with the least incentive to show a
full record. Recruitment should target creators who already show losses, wherever they are on the audience curve.
It should not chase audience size alone.

**DI-C5. Supply gate needs ~600–1,300 targeted contacts.** The pool is not binding: my low bound is about 20k public posters.
**Verdict: STANDS** as an assumption-driven estimate. The binding uncertainty is willingness to show losses, not reach.

**DI-C6. "60% of investors 18–34 use social media for investing vs 9% at 55+"; "61% made an investment decision based on a
social-media personality's recommendation."**
Opened [S5]:
- The release says "use social media", not "for investing". The investing context comes from the surrounding study, so
  the wording needs a check.
- The 61% (vs 6% at 55+) is confirmed. It matches my C10 (aged 18-34 share acting on finfluencer recommendations).

Opened [S6]: 24% of all investors and 35% of those aged 18-29 get investment information from social media. The source is a
2025 FINRA Foundation study, not the NFCS.

There are two errors:
1. [S7] is described as a "government survey". It comes from a research nonprofit.
2. The 24% figure conflicts with my 29–30% [S1]. Both are valid: they come from different surveys and different
   question wording.

**Verdict: STANDS, with corrections.** For sizing, I keep 24–30% of investors as the range, which gives 22M–27M
investor-viewers (90.8M × 24% = 21.8M). My low case moves down from 23.6M.

**DI-C12. D7 of ~20% for finance apps.** The source is an unsourced vendor blog. **Verdict: NEEDS FACT-CHECK / treat as
unsupported.** The sizing view agrees that signed-out link visitors are a weaker cohort than installs.

#### C. Conflicts and which is right
1. **trader_share:** the owner says 5–15%, DA infers 0.2–0.7% from the gate, and DI's funnel implies ~1.5–4.5%. None is
   measured. All three are assumptions; DA is wrong to treat the gate as evidence.
2. **Conversion basis:** DA is right. My SOM table repeated the stock/flow mistake.
3. **Social-media share of investors:** 24% (DI, FINRA 2025) vs 29–30% (MS, NFCS 2024). Both are correct for their
   question wording. Use the range.
4. **Supply framing:** DI says audience size drives the views gate. My view is that willingness to show losses is the
   constraint, and that it correlates inversely with audience size [MS S8]. Both are plausible; outreach data decides.

#### Items for the fact-checker
1. DA: break-even values of 2.89%, 1.78%, 5.58% at 1k viewers and the 1.18% floor (re-run `docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/model.mjs`).
2. Vendor pricing: $100 + $1/$2 per user; "personal/OAuth free for a limited time" (duration?) (confirmed 2026-11-01).
3. DA [S3]: 17.0% monthly and 44.1% yearly plans retained at 1 year (company report, not re-opened).
4. DA [S9]: $150 ad CAC, unopened, 2022. DA [S8]: ">80% organic/referral funded accounts" (SEC S-1).
5. DI [S5]: whether "60% of 18–34 use social media" refers to investing; 61% vs 6% confirmed.
6. DI [S6]: 24% / 35% aged 18-29 (FINRA Foundation 2025) confirmed; MS [S1] 29%/30%/26% (NFCS 2024 investor survey).
7. DI [S3]/[S4]: 59% never-clicked (news links, single microblog, ~2016).
8. DI C1: no referrer or page-view capture in `app/`, `lib/`, `db/schema.sql` (code check).
9. MS C2: 36% of non-retirement investors trade 4+ times/year; MS C1: 34% / 35% hold non-retirement securities.
10. MS C5/C7: 38 enabled brokerages, 3 simulated/paper; whether the sync excludes paper accounts from "Verified".
11. MS [S8]: ~29k finfluencers, 28% skilled, antiskilled attracting more followers.
12. DI [S8]: SEC 2022-221, eight traders, ~$100M; DI [S9]: 20% of finfluencer content carried disclosure.

## E2. Competitive analyst A: copy-trading apps and signal sellers (challenges: competitive C, user researcher (traders))

### P2: Cross-examination by Competitive Analyst A (copy-trading and signal sellers)

Member: Competitive Landscape Analyst A. Pass 2, 2026-11-01. Own file: p1-competitive-copy-signals.md.
Targets: p1-competitive-broker-trackers.md (BT) and p1-user-traders.md (UT). No third-party names (T1/T2/T4). New sources are [N1]..[N5].

#### A. Broker-trackers file (BT)

**BT-C1: a large commission-free app broker runs a verified in-app feed for all eligible US customers, and has 28.4M funded customers. Verdict: STANDS on the facts, WEAKENED as "the biggest threat".**
- *Verified.* The company newsroom post (2026-03-18) confirms a beta for about 1,000 invited customers, with 10,000 more "over the coming weeks". It says profiles are verified by identity check, trades are "verified live", entries and exits are shown, and 1-year and daily P&L are visible. Beta invitees were picked partly on trading activity and frequency [N1].
- *Verified.* A trade-press report dated 2026-10-14 says the feed is open to "all eligible US customers" and that the invite-only beta has ended. An executive's social-media post (2026-10-13) said it is "live for everyone". The report puts funded customers at 28.4M at end of June 2026 [N2]. A search summary of the company's Q2 2026 results release (2026-07-29) gives the same 28.4M. I could not open the primary release (403), so it goes to the fact-checker.
- *Timing nuance.* The same report says a company account had already posted on 2026-09-29 that the feature reached all customers. The "October" launch date is therefore approximate [N2].
- *What BT left out, which changes the implication:*
  - **Posting is selective.** Customers "choose which eligible trades to post", and the company's product page says "choose what you share of your own" [N2][N3].
  - **Some trades cannot be shared:** futures and multi-leg options [N2].
  - **The user agreement bars some posters:** securities-licensed professionals and people tied to a brokerage or exchange. It also says the feature "is NOT a copy trading platform" [N2].
- *What this means.* The feed verifies each trade but not the record. It shows a trader's best trades with a verified label, which is the selective-disclosure pattern behind the enforcement record in my C9. A "verified" badge is now free for 28.4M people. Stock Social's wedge therefore shifts from "verified" to "whole record": TR-1 (no per-trade hiding), losses ranked like gains, and intervals. It is not "multi-broker" either.
- *Overstated parts.* 28.4M is the eligible base, not participation, and no adoption figure exists [N2]. No source says whether profiles can be seen on the open web; the product page shows only log-in and sign-up links [N3].
- *Revised claim.* "A broker with 28.4M funded customers (June 2026) offers a free, identity-verified, *trader-selected* trade feed inside its app. It is the main threat to the *verified label*, not to the whole-record product." Confidence: High on the facts, Medium on the implication.

**BT-C2: the copy-trading broker pays traders 1.5%/yr of AUC; "the signals seller can earn from a verified record elsewhere today". Verdict: WEAKENED.**
- The rates match my S2.
- **BT's "40M+ registered users" is the wrong base.** The prospectus counts about 3.3K paid traders, about 0.1% of *funded* accounts. The FY2025 figure is 3.81M funded accounts [my S1, S3].
- **Earnings are small for nearly everyone.** By my C1 estimate, an entry-tier trader ($50K AUC) earns about $750/yr.
- **Entry is outcome-screened and exclusive.** Traders need at least 30% profitable months, a drawdown cap and an exclusivity agreement [my S2].
- **US availability is unclear.** BT says the page shows no US entity among the program's regulated entities. If so, US-based sellers may not be able to earn from it at all (NEEDS FACT-CHECK).
- *Revised.* "A tiny, outcome-screened tail of traders earns from a single-ledger record, possibly not US residents." The positioning conclusion stands: Stock Social never pays.

**BT-C5 and its synthesis: social-investing apps could copy multi-broker records "in weeks"; journals are the fastest serious entrant. Verdict: STANDS (estimate). This conflicts in part with my C13.**
- I named strategy and signal marketplaces as the likeliest fast follower ("linked external account" badges).
- BT is more convincing for *data capability*: journals already sync 500-900+ brokers (claimed).
- Mine is more convincing for *motive*: marketplaces sell records to buyers, while journal buyers want privacy (BT concedes this).
- Merged view: journals can build it soonest, and marketplaces have the most reason to. Neither has whole-record rules. Confidence: Medium.

**BT-C8: forex track-record verification exists since 2009. Verdict: STANDS, but the source is stale (2010).**
- My C7 gives a *current* analogue: a forex signal marketplace's rules [my S10]. It accepts real accounts only, verifies through a read-only investor password, shows balance, equity, deposits and P/L publicly, and takes a 20% commission.
- That supports BT's point that the "verified badge plus equity curve" pattern is familiar. It also shows the pattern is built to sell copying, which Stock Social forbids.
- Use my S10 as the current evidence.

**BT-C9 and C12 (vendor).** Outside my scope. I agree with both. My C13 cost estimate ($1/user/month + $100/month) matches BT-C9.

#### B. User-traders file (UT)

**UT-C1: most records will be negative or show no clear pattern. Verdict: STANDS (numbers verified), with one transfer caveat.**
- I opened the abstract [N4]. For traders who started in 2013-2015 and persisted at least 300 days: 97% lost money, 0.4% earned more than a bank teller (US$54/day), and the top individual earned US$310/day.
- *Caveat.* That sample is futures day traders, not US equity/ETF swing traders, so size transfer stays at Medium, as UT says.
- The enforcement cases in my C9 show the paid-seller segment is where claimed results diverge most from real ones. That strengthens UT's point that the honesty rules will expose it.

**UT-C4: publishing P&L is tied to selling access, and enforcement exists. Verdict: STANDS, and is under-evidenced.**
- UT cites two cases. My C9 adds three findings that bear directly on design:
  - A CFTC case where a promoter pledged to "post all trades" but published only aggregate profits.
  - An FTC settlement ($2.425M) with a service that "did not track subscriber results".
  - A 17-regulator finfluencer action (2026-04).
- Each failure is selective or aggregate disclosure, which TR-1..TR-8 prevent.
- *New point from BT-C1.* The broker feed's ban on licensed professionals leaves *unlicensed* sellers (the ICP-S1 pattern) free to post selectively there. Stock Social would be the stricter venue. That is good for viewer trust and bad for S1 recruiting.

**UT-C7: FINRA Foundation figures. Verdict: STANDS (verified).**
- I extracted the brief's text [N5]. It confirms:
  - 29% of retail investors use social media for investment decisions (60% among ages 18-34).
  - 26% acted on a finfluencer's recommendation (61% among 18-34).
  - Among those targeted for fraud, 68-69% of users lost money, against 26-29% of non-users.
  - Entertainment as a motive: 59% vs 18%. Social activity: 59% vs 11%.
- The source is the 2024 NFCS Investor Study, published April 2026.

**UT-C9 H3: the share rate is about 55%, below the 60% gate. Verdict: WEAKENED.**
- The arithmetic is right: 0.15×0.8 + 0.85×0.5 = 0.545.
- The inputs are not reliable. The 15% comes from a snippet UT did not open (Low), and 80%/50% are assumptions.
- If the non-edge share rate is 55% instead of 50%, the result is about 59%. At 57%, it is about 60%. The conclusion is inside the noise.
- *Revised.* "Untested; the gate is sensitive to the share rate of non-winning records. Measure it." UT's qualitative point (recruiting mix decides the gate) stands.
- New competitive pressure: the broker feed now lets S2 hobbyists post wins only, for free, to an audience in the app. That lowers S2's reason to share a whole record here.

**UT-C10: the realistic first 50. Verdict: STANDS. It agrees with my C12 and is reinforced.**
- S1 is a poor early segment for several reasons:
  - It sells forward alerts, which F3b bans.
  - It competes with venues that *pay*: 1.5% of AUC, 80% of signal fees, 10-50% profit shares [my C10].
  - Its counsel status is open.
- I would add one candidate: forex and signal-marketplace sellers already used to read-only verification [my S10]. This only works if the data vendor covers their brokers, which is unknown (my open question 2).

#### Conflicts resolved
- **Base for the copy-trading broker:** use funded accounts (3.81M) and the 0.1% paid share (mine). BT's 40M registered users is the wrong base.
- **Fast follower:** journals have the capability (BT), marketplaces have the motive (mine). Keep both.
- **Wedge:** BT's "multi-broker plus open-web link" and my "whole record" fit together. After N2, whole-record rigor is the primary wedge: "verified" alone is now free and selective at scale.

#### Items for the fact-checker
1. **28.4M funded customers at 2026-06-30, app broker.** Check the Q2 2026 results release (2026-07-29) or the 10-Q (SEC EDGAR). The primary page returned 403.
2. **Broker feed open to "all eligible US customers" on 2026-10-13/14.** Also check the earlier 2026-09-29 "all customers" statement and whether profiles can be seen on the open web.
3. **The feed's user agreement (effective 2026-09-18).** Check selective posting, the exclusion of licensed professionals, the "NOT a copy trading platform" wording and any age limit.
4. **Copy-trading broker counts.** Check 3.81M funded accounts, "over 5,000" program members (FY2025), and about 3.3K paid traders at about 0.1% of funded accounts (2024 prospectus).
5. **Whether that broker's trader program is open to US residents** (BT-C2).
6. **The 15% "positive edge" share in UT-C9** (unopened snippet), which drives the 55% estimate.
7. **Data vendor coverage of forex and crypto venues** (my open question 2; BT-C10's "40+ institutions").
8. **The data vendor's activities polling limit (once per account per 24h) against the hourly sync** (BT-C12).
9. **The signal marketplace's 20% commission and the strategy marketplace's 70-87% creator share** (my C7, C8). Benchmarks b07 and b08 remain unverified.

#### New sources (opened: yes unless stated)
- [N1] Company newsroom post, the commission-free app broker's social beta, 2026-03-18; URL withheld (T1).
- [N2] Trade-press article, the same broker's feed opened to all eligible US customers, 2026-10-14; URL withheld.
- [N3] Company product page for the same feed, accessed 2026-11-01; URL withheld.
- [N4] Working paper abstract, "Day trading for a living?", 2019. https://econpapers.repec.org/paper/spawpaper/2019wpecon47.htm
- [N5] FINRA Foundation research brief, April 2026 (2024 NFCS Investor Study). https://finrafoundation.org/sites/finrafoundation/files/2026-03/FINRA_Foundation_Research_Brief_Social_Media_Finfluencers.pdf
- Q2 2026 results release of the app broker, 2026-07-29: seen in search summary only; opened: no (403).

## E3. Competitive analyst B: social investing feeds, forums, creators (challenges: distribution, user researcher (viewers))

### P2: Cross-examination by Competitive Landscape Analyst B (social feeds, forums, finance creators)

**Member:** Competitive Landscape Analyst B. **Pass:** 2. **Date:** 2026-11-01. **Targets:** p1-distribution.md (D), p1-user-viewers.md (V). **Own file:** p1-competitive-social-forums.md (B).
Fetches this pass: 5 opened. I also re-read the repo's `db/schema.sql`, `app/sitemap.ts` and `lib/referral.ts`, plus `docs/direction/market-2026-11-01/projection-assumptions.json`.

#### Target 1: p1-distribution.md

**D-C1 (the gates cannot be measured as built). STANDS.**
I checked this independently. The repo has no handling of the Referer header for profile requests. The only view counters, `users.record_*_viewed_at` and `record_view_count`, count a trader opening their *own* `/me/record`. `funnel_events.own_profile_viewed` is also a self-view, recorded during signup. There is no external-view table. Revised claim: the views gate and the D7 gate cannot be known. The share gate is partly knowable, because a signup counted by `lib/referral.ts` proves at least one share, but it under-counts shares. *High.*

**D-C2 (passive broadcast is the stronger lever; analogue = bio link + pinned post). WEAKENED.**
I re-opened the abstract. The figures are confirmed: +246% for passive broadcast, a further +98% for personalized features, and broadcast "used more often, generating more total peer adoption" [S1]. The analogy is the problem. In that experiment the broadcast was an automatic notification *inside* the host network. A bio link or a pinned post that points off-platform is the opposite case. On a large general network, posts with an external link were only 1.1% of US feed views in Q2 2026. On a microblog, link posts had median visibility 4.7-7.5× lower than posts without links (B-C9). On the short-video platform, a bio link may not exist below a follower threshold (B-C10, unconfirmed). Revised claim: "broadcast" beats "invite" only when the platform distributes the broadcast. For Stock Social the closest real equivalent is a native card *image* posted with the link in a reply or the bio. A bare link post is not equivalent. *Medium.*

**D-C3 (heavy tail; 59% of shared links never clicked). STANDS, NEEDS FACT-CHECK on the number.**
This direction matches B-C9. The 59% comes second-hand: the primary paper was not opened, and the figure is from 2016 and about news links. It should not be used as a rate in the arithmetic. *Medium.*

**D-C4 (funnel: the gate needs about 10 ICP-S1 traders with 10k-50k audiences). WEAKENED on two counts.**
(a) **The reach assumption is optimistic for link posts.** The assumption that 5-15% of the audience sees a post is borrowed from organic reach in general. The measured 4.7-7.5× penalty on link posts (B-C9) would cut it to roughly 1-3% (*estimate*: 10% ÷ 4.7-7.5 ≈ 1.3-2.1%). At that reach, D's 20 views per post for a 10k-follower seller becomes about 3-4. On these assumptions the gap to 5,000/week grows from D's "3-10×" to as much as about 20-50×. That makes chat-group links and bio links the dominant terms, and they are exactly the ones D could not source.
(b) **The proposed fix conflicts with regulation and with self-selection.** "Deliberately recruit ICP-S1 signal sellers" collides with B-C7. ESMA treats follower-driven, monetized posting as a possible *professional* recommendation, and FCA FG24/1 can make unauthorised promotion a criminal matter. Verifying such a seller's record could look like the platform lending them credibility. B-C4 and B-C12 add that the most-followed accounts are on average *less* skilled, and so gain least from a full record that includes losses. D's own channel #1 therefore depends on the segment least likely to opt in and most exposed legally. Revised claim: the views gate is very unlikely to be met organically by week 8 under any cohort mix we can source. ICP-S1 recruitment needs the legal committee's view before it becomes the plan. *Medium (direction), Low (size).*

**D-C6 (demand on image-first channels; microblog at 31% among 18-29). STANDS, with an omission.**
The national research organization confirmed the 18-29 figures: long-video 95%, photo app 79%, short-video 65%, microblog 31% (Jan 30-Jun 18 2026, n=5,511) [S2]. D left out that the large discussion-board community reaches 40% of adults 18-29 [S2]. That platform is also the second-ranked channel overall for finfluencer followers (B-C2). The forum thread, where a link unfurls in context, deserves a place in D's channel list. It currently has none. *High (data).*

**D-C12 (D7 ~20% for finance apps). NEEDS FACT-CHECK.**
The source is a vendor blog with no stated data, so it should not anchor a kill gate. I agree with D's suggestion to split signed-in D7 from all-visitor D7. *Low.*

**Conflict D vs B:** D ranks "Share-card unfurl" as channel #2 and treats link sharing as the core loop. B argues the link is the weakest format on feeds and the card's best use is as a native image. B is better evidenced: it cites a platform transparency report and a 34.8M-post study, while D relies on an analogy from 2011. Both files agree that unfurls in chat and threads are an unmeasured assumption.

#### Target 2: p1-user-viewers.md

**V-C1 (social media is a mainstream channel; UK 62%/74%/9 in 10). STANDS, with a caveat.**
The US figures check against the FINRA Foundation brief (text extracted this pass) [S3]. The FCA release confirms 62% of 18-29s follow influencers, 74% of those trust their advice, and 9 in 10 were encouraged to change behaviour. It names no survey and does not say the behaviour is *investing* [S4], so Medium is the right label. A caveat for V and for my own B-C1: the brief says its wording "is unable to distinguish" social media use from message boards [S3]. The 29%/60% figures therefore measure forums and feeds together. That actually *raises* the weight of discussion boards for this product. *High (US).*

**V-C3 (due diligence is high and fraud losses are higher, which is the paying job). STANDS on data; the implication is WEAKENED.**
Verified: 7.6 vs 4.0 sources, and 36% vs 14% checking a professional's background [S3]. The FTC spotlight confirms $2.1B lost in 2025 to scams that started on social media, $1.1B of it investment scams, including chat groups of fake "successful investors" and fake platforms showing fake profits [S5]. The $5.7B 2024 figure was not on that page and was not opened by V, so it still needs checking. The weakness is the job statement: "check a record that cannot be faked" only helps if the person making the claim is on Stock Social. The fraud described runs through fake platforms and private chats, and those actors will never connect. V-C9 already says this, and V-C3 should carry the same limit. *Medium.*

**V-C4 (entertainment and relatability drive viewing; D7 depends on the thesis layer). STANDS.**
This matches B-C3. It adds a competitive point V did not draw out. On entertainment, Stock Social competes with long-video and short-video creators, who post daily. A feed of closed trades from 50 traders is thin supply for that job. *Medium.*

**V-C8 (base conversion 1.5% vs break-even 1.7-2.8%). STANDS as arithmetic.**
`projection-assumptions.json` confirms base 0.015, conservative 0.005 and strong 0.03. The basis given is benchmarks from app downloads and newsletters, not finance-viewer data. From my side: paid author marketplaces on research/forum sites compete for the same "pay for judgment" wallet (B-C12), and none of them was priced. This is a missing comparator for the $9.99 price. *Medium.*

**V-C9 (the record a viewer wants is not on the platform). STANDS, and B strengthens it.**
B-C4 and B-C12 give the mechanism: popularity is inversely related to skill, so high-audience sellers have the most to lose from a full record. V-C9 and D-C4 directly contradict each other. D's growth plan depends on ICP-S1 joining, while V's demand analysis says ICP-S1 has every reason not to. B sides with V. The council should treat ICP-S1 uptake as the main unverified assumption, because DESIGN's ICP-S1 interviews are unrecorded.

#### Items for the fact-checker
1. 1.1% of US feed views being link posts in Q2 2026, and 1.3% in Q1 (B-C9, company transparency report).
2. Median visibility 4.7× and 7.5× lower for link posts (B-C9, arXiv 2410.17390). Check whether the comparison is against news links only.
3. Bio-link eligibility of about 1,000 followers plus a business account on the short-video platform (B-C10; the official page did not render).
4. 59% of shared URLs never clicked (D-C3; the primary 2016 paper was not opened).
5. Finance-app D7 of about 20% and all-app D7 of 10.9% (D-C12; vendor blog with no data source).
6. "Over 80% of new funded accounts organic/referral" from a broker filing (D-C5, projection b28; not re-opened by anyone).
7. $5.7B in 2024 US investment-scam losses (V-C3; the FTC page was not opened).
8. Fintech cost per registration of about $18 (D worst channels, projection b27).
9. The finfluencer skill split: 28% skilled / 16% unskilled / 56% antiskilled, about ±2.5%/month (B-C4, V-C2).
10. 18-34 reliance of 60% and 61% and channel shares 77/51/49/47/46/40/25% (B-C1/C2, V-C1). The text was extracted and the totals match; confirm the channel table.
11. Week-8 gate arithmetic: 5,000 ÷ 30 sharers ≈ 167 views each per week (B-C11) vs D's ~750/week at 25 views per share.
12. ESMA's EUR 500,000 natural-person sanction for breaches of the recommendation regime (B-C7).

#### Sources (this pass)
- [S1] Randomized field experiment on viral product design, academic journal, 2011. https://ideas.repec.org/a/inm/ormnsc/v57y2011i9p1623-1639.html. Opened: yes (abstract).
- [S2] a national nonpartisan research organization social media fact sheet, survey Jan 30-Jun 18 2026. (URL omitted per T1) Opened: yes.
- [S3] FINRA Investor Education Foundation research brief on social media users and finfluencer followers, Apr 2026. https://finrafoundation.org/sites/finrafoundation/files/2026-03/FINRA_Foundation_Research_Brief_Social_Media_Finfluencers.pdf. Opened: yes (text extracted).
- [S4] FCA press release on illegal finfluencers, 2024-10-22. https://www.fca.org.uk/news/press-releases/fca-cracks-down-illegal-finfluencers. Opened: yes.
